"""What the gate is allowed to run, what it must never run, and what it does when time runs out.

The safety law under test: a command that is not on the allowlist never reaches a shell. Two
cases prove it with a sentinel file that would be deleted if the law broke, one for a row the
model marked ``manual`` and one for a row marked ``auto`` whose command stopped being allowlisted
after the rules changed.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import shutil
import subprocess
import time
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import pytest

from lib import proofs

# why: the shipped allowlist in algorithm/rules.json, spelled here so a rules edit
# cannot quietly change what these cases mean; one test below reads the real file and agrees.
ALLOW = [
    "pytest",
    "python",
    "python3",
    "grep",
    "rg",
    "git",
    "ls",
    "test",
    "node",
    "npm test",
    "curl http://localhost",
    "curl http://127.0.0.1",
]

EXIT_OK = 'python -c "import sys; sys.exit(0)"'  # why: the smallest passing proof that needs no files
# why: fails and says something, so the output capture is testable
EXIT_FAIL = 'python -c "print(\'boom\'); import sys; sys.exit(1)"'
SLEEP_FIVE = 'python -c "import time; time.sleep(5)"'  # why: outlives any per-proof cap set here
# why: three of these outrun a 1.5 s whole-run budget
SLEEP_ONE = 'python -c "import time; time.sleep(1)"'

# why: Git Bash on Windows takes about half a second to start a login shell, so a bound of three
# seconds still proves the five-second sleep was cut short without turning startup jitter into a red.
TIMEOUT_BOUND_SECONDS = 3.0
# why: two of the three one-second sleeps run before the budget is spent; four seconds covers both
# plus their shells and still fails if the third one ran.
BUDGET_BOUND_SECONDS = 12.0

has_bash = pytest.mark.skipif(shutil.which("bash") is None, reason="bash is not on PATH")
has_git = pytest.mark.skipif(shutil.which("git") is None, reason="git is not on PATH")


def a_row(
    row_id: str = "R1",
    text: str = "the tests pass",
    proof: str = EXIT_OK,
    kind: str = "auto",
    status: str = "○",
    struck: bool = False,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """One ledger row, built by hand: this module never imports the ledger it is handed."""
    return {
        "id": row_id,
        "text": text,
        "proof": proof,
        "kind": kind,
        "status": status,
        "struck": struck,
        "history": [],
        "last_output": "",
        "last_run_at": None,
    }


def a_ledger(*rows: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    return {"contract_id": "0042", "title": "members area", "rows": list(rows)}


def some_rules(
    allow: list[str] | None = None, per_proof: float = 15, total: float = 45
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    return {
        "proofs": {
            "allow_prefixes": ALLOW if allow is None else allow,
            "per_proof_seconds": per_proof,
            "total_seconds": total,
        }
    }


def only_these(*names: str):
    """A ``shutil.which`` that finds only the named programs."""

    def fake_which(command: str, *_args: Any, **_kwargs: Any) -> str | None:
        return f"/fake/{command}" if command in names else None

    return fake_which


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("command", "allowed"),
    [
        (EXIT_OK, True),
        ("pythonx -c 'nope'", False),
        ("npm test --x", True),
        ("npm install", False),
        ("curl http://localhost:3000", True),
        ("curl http://evil.example", False),
        ("rm -rf /", False),
    ],
)
def test_is_allowlisted_table(command: str, allowed: bool) -> None:
    assert proofs.is_allowlisted(command, ALLOW) is allowed


def test_is_allowlisted_reads_a_prefix_only_on_a_word_boundary() -> None:
    assert proofs.is_allowlisted("curl http://localhost", ALLOW) is True
    assert proofs.is_allowlisted("curl http://localhost.evil.com", ALLOW) is False
    assert proofs.is_allowlisted("", ALLOW) is False
    assert proofs.is_allowlisted(EXIT_OK, []) is False


def test_the_shipped_rules_carry_the_same_allowlist() -> None:
    from lib import rules as rules_module

    allow = rules_module.load_rules()["proofs"]["allow_prefixes"]
    assert allow == ALLOW
    assert proofs.is_allowlisted(EXIT_OK, allow) is True
    assert proofs.is_allowlisted("rm -rf /", allow) is False


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "proof, command, lines",
    [
        ("grep -r '$29' demo/site → 0", "grep -r '$29' demo/site", 0),
        ("git diff demo/site/checkout -> 0", "git diff demo/site/checkout", 0),
        ("ls demo → 3", "ls demo", 3),
        ("python -c \"print(1)\"", "python -c \"print(1)\"", None),
    ],
)
def test_split_expectation_reads_the_arrow(proof: str, command: str, lines: int | None) -> None:
    assert proofs.split_expectation(proof) == (command, lines)


def test_the_arrow_never_hides_a_command_from_the_allowlist() -> None:
    assert proofs.is_allowlisted("grep -r x → 0", ["grep"])
    assert not proofs.is_allowlisted("rm -rf x → 0", ["grep"])


def test_an_arrow_proof_passes_on_the_line_count_not_the_exit_code(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    (tmp_path / "a.txt").write_text("alpha" + chr(10), encoding="utf-8")
    # grep exits 1 when nothing matches, and that is exactly the pass the arrow asks for
    status, output, _ = proofs.run_one("grep zzz a.txt → 0", str(tmp_path), 15)
    assert status == "●"
    assert output.startswith("0 lines, wanted 0")
    status, output, _ = proofs.run_one("grep alpha a.txt → 0", str(tmp_path), 15)
    assert status == "✗"
    assert output.startswith("1 lines, wanted 0")
    status, _, _ = proofs.run_one("grep alpha a.txt → 1", str(tmp_path), 15)
    assert status == "●"
    # a missing path makes grep exit 2 and print one error line: that line is never a match
    status, output, _ = proofs.run_one("grep alpha nowhere/ → 1", str(tmp_path), 15)
    assert status == "✗"
    status, output, _ = proofs.run_one("grep alpha nowhere/ → 0", str(tmp_path), 15)
    assert status == "✗"


def test_a_passing_proof_is_stamped_proven(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    results = proofs.run_all(a_ledger(a_row()), str(tmp_path), some_rules())

    assert len(results) == 1
    assert results[0].row_id == "R1"
    assert results[0].status == "●"
    assert results[0].command == EXIT_OK
    assert results[0].seconds > 0


def test_a_failing_proof_is_stamped_failed_and_keeps_its_output(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    results = proofs.run_all(a_ledger(a_row(proof=EXIT_FAIL)), str(tmp_path), some_rules())

    assert results[0].status == "✗"
    assert "boom" in results[0].output


def test_a_manual_row_is_never_executed(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    sentinel = tmp_path / "keep" / "sentinel.txt"
    sentinel.parent.mkdir()
    sentinel.write_text("still here\n", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n")
    row = a_row(proof=f'rm -rf "{sentinel.parent.as_posix()}"', kind="manual")

    results = proofs.run_all(a_ledger(row), str(tmp_path), some_rules())

    assert results[0].status == "manual"
    assert results[0].output == ""
    assert results[0].seconds == 0.0
    assert sentinel.exists()


def test_an_auto_row_that_left_the_allowlist_is_never_executed(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    sentinel = tmp_path / "keep" / "sentinel.txt"
    sentinel.parent.mkdir()
    sentinel.write_text("still here\n", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n")
    row = a_row(proof=f'rm -rf "{sentinel.parent.as_posix()}"', kind="auto")

    results = proofs.run_all(a_ledger(row), str(tmp_path), some_rules(allow=["pytest"]))

    assert results[0].status == "manual"
    assert sentinel.exists()


def test_a_struck_row_is_skipped(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    ledger = a_ledger(
        a_row("R1", proof=EXIT_OK),
        a_row("R2", proof=EXIT_FAIL, status="●", struck=True),
    )

    results = proofs.run_all(ledger, str(tmp_path), some_rules())

    assert [result.row_id for result in results] == ["R1"]


def test_a_slow_proof_times_out_and_is_cut_short(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    started = time.monotonic()
    results = proofs.run_all(
        a_ledger(a_row(proof=SLEEP_FIVE)), str(tmp_path), some_rules(per_proof=1)
    )
    elapsed = time.monotonic() - started

    assert results[0].status == "⏱"
    assert results[0].output == "timed out after 1s"
    assert elapsed < TIMEOUT_BOUND_SECONDS


def test_the_whole_run_budget_stops_the_last_proof_before_it_starts(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    # why: R1 is quick, R2 sleeps past the whole-run budget, so R3 must be refused before it
    # starts; the budget (3 s) sits above a cold login-shell start under load, so R2 always begins
    ledger = a_ledger(
        a_row("R1", proof=EXIT_OK),
        a_row("R2", proof=SLEEP_FIVE),
        a_row("R3", proof=EXIT_OK),
    )

    started = time.monotonic()
    results = proofs.run_all(ledger, str(tmp_path), some_rules(per_proof=15, total=3))
    elapsed = time.monotonic() - started

    assert [result.status for result in results] == ["●", "●", "⏱"]
    assert results[2].seconds == 0.0
    assert results[2].output == "not run: the proof run passed its 3s budget"
    assert elapsed < BUDGET_BOUND_SECONDS


@has_bash
def test_choose_shell_picks_bash_when_it_is_on_path() -> None:
    name, argv = proofs.choose_shell()

    assert name == "bash"
    assert argv[1] == "-lc"
    assert Path(argv[0]).name.lower().startswith("bash")

    assert proofs.choose_shell() == (
        "powershell",
        ["/fake/powershell", "-NoProfile", "-Command"],
    )


def test_choose_shell_falls_back_to_cmd(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
    monkeypatch.setattr(proofs.shutil, "which", only_these("cmd"))

    assert proofs.choose_shell() == ("cmd", ["/fake/cmd", "/c"])


def test_choose_shell_says_so_when_there_is_no_shell(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
    monkeypatch.setattr(proofs.shutil, "which", only_these())

    assert proofs.choose_shell() == ("none", [])


def test_run_one_without_a_shell_fails_without_running(
    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
) -> None:
    monkeypatch.setattr(proofs.shutil, "which", only_these())

    status, output, seconds = proofs.run_one(EXIT_OK, str(tmp_path), 5)

    assert (status, output, seconds) == ("✗", "no shell found on PATH", 0.0)


@has_bash

    assert status == "●"
    assert "a b" in output


def test_run_one_runs_in_the_folder_it_is_given(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    (tmp_path / "marker.txt").write_text("here\n", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n")

    status, output, _seconds = proofs.run_one(
        'python -c "import os; print(os.path.exists(\'marker.txt\'))"', str(tmp_path), 15
    )

    assert status == "●"
    assert "True" in output


def a_git_repo(path: Path) -> None:
    """A real repository with one commit, so ``git status --porcelain`` has a baseline."""
    git = shutil.which("git")
    assert git is not None
    subprocess.run([git, "init"], cwd=path, capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
    (path / "seed.txt").write_text("seed\n", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n")
    subprocess.run([git, "add", "seed.txt"], cwd=path, capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
    subprocess.run(
        [
            git,
            "-c",
            "user.email=test@example.com",
            "-c",
            "user.name=Test",
            "commit",
            "--no-verify",
            "-m",
            "seed",
        ],
        cwd=path,
        capture_output=True,
        text=True,
        check=True,
    )


@has_git
def test_git_touched_outside_lists_a_file_no_row_names(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    a_git_repo(tmp_path)
    (tmp_path / "outside.txt").write_text("new\n", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n")

    touched = proofs.git_touched_outside(str(tmp_path), a_ledger(a_row()))

    assert touched == ["outside.txt"]

================================================
FILE: tests/test_report.py
================================================
"""The golden set and the report that grades the algorithm against it.

Two halves. The arithmetic is graded on a six-line fixture with a stand-in decider, so these tests
say whether accuracy, precision, recall, the confusion table and the miss ranking are right without
depending on the scoring modules. The golden set itself is graded as data: the counts the plan asks
for, the closed voice set, no demo prompt, and every explicit token exercised. The one test that
runs the real algorithm over the real golden set is skipped until ``lib.signals`` and
``score.decide`` are on disk, and it checks the shape of the number rather than the number: the
0.90 bar is enforced by the report's own exit code.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import importlib.util
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import pytest

# why: tests/ sits directly under the repo root, where the `router` package lives
WS_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
if str(WS_ROOT) not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, str(WS_ROOT))


def _load_report() -> Any:
    """Import ``router/report.py`` by path, as module ``router_report``.

    By path for the same reason conftest loads the hooks that way: ``.claude/hooks`` sits at the
    front of ``sys.path`` and holds a ``router.py``, so a plain ``import router`` finds the hook
    rather than the package. The CLI itself is run as ``python -m router.report`` from the repo
    root, where the package wins.
    """
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("router_report", WS_ROOT / "router" / "report.py")
    if spec is None or spec.loader is None:
        raise ImportError("cannot load router/report.py")
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    sys.modules[spec.name] = module
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    return module


report = _load_report()

# why: the eight prompts the demo walk types; the golden set proves the algorithm on other words
DEMO_PROMPTS = (
    "should I post the graph reel on tiktok too or keep it on instagram",
    "where are we on the members area",
    "ok the members area needs to work on mobile, email-only login, $47 everywhere no $29 left, "
    "don't touch checkout, skool opens a new tab, feels premium, due friday",
    "go",
    "where were we",
    "what time is it /e5",
    "receipt",
    "oh fuck you claude",
)

# why: the closed register set every golden line is written in
VOICES = frozenset({"blunt", "upbeat", "polite", "terse", "typo", "long"})

# why: the plan's counts for the golden set
MIN_LINES = 120
MIN_PER_SKIN = 15
MIN_QUESTIONS = 20
MIN_RANTS = 10
MIN_BANTER = 10
MIN_ACKS = 10
MIN_AMBIGUOUS = 10

# why: signals.question_shape's own first-token set, so the question count here means what it means there
QUESTION_WORDS = frozenset(
    {
        "should", "is", "are", "can", "do", "does", "did", "which", "what",
        "how", "why", "would", "could", "will", "where", "when", "who",
    }
)

# why: the eight skins in the shipped rules' own order, so the fixture's tables read like the real ones
FIXTURE_RULES: dict[str, Any] = {
    "default_skin": "bluf",
    "skins": {
        "minimal": {"tier": "E1"},
        "bluf": {"tier": "E2"},
        "sitrep": {"tier": "E3"},
        "contract": {"tier": "E4"},
        "bars": {"tier": "E4"},
        "board": {"tier": "E3"},
        "receipt": {"tier": "E2"},
        "banter": {"tier": "E1"},
    },
}

FIXTURE_LINES: list[dict[str, Any]] = [
    {"prompt": "thanks", "skin": "minimal", "voice": "polite"},
    {
        "prompt": "where are we on the pricing page",
        "skin": "sitrep",
        "voice": "blunt",
        "state": {"ledger_on_disk": True},
    },
    {"prompt": "should we launch on a saturday or a weekday?", "skin": "bluf", "voice": "blunt"},
    {"prompt": "show me the board", "skin": "board", "voice": "blunt"},
    {"prompt": "receipt please", "skin": "receipt", "voice": "polite"},
    {"prompt": "you're useless", "skin": "banter", "voice": "blunt"},
]

# why: four right and two wrong, so accuracy, precision and recall each have a number to be wrong about
FIXTURE_PREDICTIONS = {
    "thanks": "minimal",
    "where are we on the pricing page": "sitrep",
    "should we launch on a saturday or a weekday?": "bluf",
    "show me the board": "board",
    "receipt please": "bluf",
    "you're useless": "minimal",
}

# why: the wide miss and the near miss, so the ranking has something to sort
FIXTURE_SCORES = {
    "receipt please": [("bluf", 0.80), ("receipt", 0.10)],
    "you're useless": [("minimal", 0.40), ("banter", 0.35)],
}


@dataclass
class FakeDecision:
    """What the report reads off a decision: the skin, the score and where it came from."""

    skin: str
    tier: str = "E2"
    score: float = 0.5
    source: str = "rules"
    signals_top3: list[tuple[str, float]] = field(default_factory=list)
    tie_with: str | None = None


@pytest.fixture
def fixture_report(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> report.Report:
    """The six-line fixture graded with a stand-in decider."""

    def fake_decide(line: dict[str, Any], rules: dict[str, Any], classifier: str = "rules") -> FakeDecision:
        return FakeDecision(skin=FIXTURE_PREDICTIONS[line["prompt"]])

    def fake_scores(
        line: dict[str, Any], rules: dict[str, Any], classifier: str = "rules"
    ) -> list[tuple[str, float]]:
        return FIXTURE_SCORES.get(line["prompt"], [])

    monkeypatch.setattr(report, "decide", fake_decide)
    monkeypatch.setattr(report, "skin_scores", fake_scores)
    return report.evaluate(FIXTURE_LINES, FIXTURE_RULES)


def test_accuracy_counts_the_lines_that_landed(fixture_report: report.Report) -> None:
    assert fixture_report.total == len(FIXTURE_LINES)
    assert fixture_report.correct == 4
    assert fixture_report.accuracy == pytest.approx(4 / 6)


def test_confusion_holds_every_line_once(fixture_report: report.Report) -> None:
    cells = sum(sum(row.values()) for row in fixture_report.confusion.values())
    assert cells == len(FIXTURE_LINES)
    assert fixture_report.confusion["receipt"]["bluf"] == 1
    assert fixture_report.confusion["banter"]["minimal"] == 1
    assert fixture_report.confusion["board"]["board"] == 1
    assert fixture_report.confusion["receipt"]["receipt"] == 0


def test_confusion_labels_follow_the_rules_file(fixture_report: report.Report) -> None:
    assert fixture_report.labels == list(FIXTURE_RULES["skins"])


def test_precision_and_recall_per_skin(fixture_report: report.Report) -> None:
    bluf = fixture_report.per_skin["bluf"]
    assert (bluf.support, bluf.predicted, bluf.correct) == (1, 2, 1)
    assert bluf.precision == pytest.approx(0.5)
    assert bluf.recall == pytest.approx(1.0)

    receipt = fixture_report.per_skin["receipt"]
    assert (receipt.support, receipt.predicted, receipt.correct) == (1, 0, 0)
    assert receipt.precision == 0.0
    assert receipt.recall == 0.0

    contract = fixture_report.per_skin["contract"]
    assert (contract.support, contract.predicted, contract.correct) == (0, 0, 0)
    assert contract.precision == 0.0


def test_worst_misses_lead_with_the_widest_gap(fixture_report: report.Report) -> None:
    assert [miss.prompt for miss in fixture_report.misses] == ["receipt please", "you're useless"]
    widest = fixture_report.misses[0]
    assert (widest.expected, widest.predicted) == ("receipt", "bluf")
    assert widest.gap == pytest.approx(0.70)
    assert widest.top2 == [("bluf", 0.80), ("receipt", 0.10)]
    assert fixture_report.misses[1].gap == pytest.approx(0.05)


def test_a_skin_outside_the_rules_still_gets_a_column(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
    monkeypatch.setattr(
        report, "decide", lambda line, rules, classifier="rules": FakeDecision(skin="haiku-invented")
    )
    monkeypatch.setattr(report, "skin_scores", lambda line, rules, classifier="rules": [])
    result = report.evaluate(FIXTURE_LINES[:1], FIXTURE_RULES)
    assert result.labels[-1] == "haiku-invented"
    assert result.confusion["minimal"]["haiku-invented"] == 1
    assert result.accuracy == 0.0
    assert result.misses[0].top2 == []


def test_format_report_prints_the_three_tables(fixture_report: report.Report) -> None:
    text = report.format_report(fixture_report)
    assert "top-1 accuracy: 0.6667" in text
    assert "PER SKIN" in text
    assert "CONFUSION" in text
    assert "WORST MISSES  2 of 2" in text
    assert "wanted receipt, got bluf" in text
    assert "bluf 0.80 · receipt 0.10" in text
    assert max(len(line) for line in text.splitlines()) <= 88


def test_the_bar_is_the_plans_bar() -> None:
    assert report.ACCURACY_BAR == 0.90


def test_load_golden_names_the_line_it_could_not_read(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    path = tmp_path / "broken.jsonl"
    path.write_text(
        '{"prompt": "thanks", "skin": "minimal", "voice": "blunt"}\nnot json\n',
        encoding="utf-8",
        newline="\n",
    )
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="line 2"):
        report.load_golden(path)


def test_load_golden_refuses_an_unknown_state_key(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    path = tmp_path / "state.jsonl"
    path.write_text(
        '{"prompt": "go!", "skin": "bars", "voice": "blunt", "state": {"ledger_on_dsik": true}}\n',
        encoding="utf-8",
        newline="\n",
    )
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="ledger_on_dsik"):
        report.load_golden(path)


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def golden() -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    """The shipped golden set."""
    return report.load_golden(report.GOLDEN_PATH)


def test_golden_set_is_big_enough(golden: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
    assert len(golden) >= MIN_LINES


def test_golden_set_covers_every_skin(golden: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
    from lib.rules import load_rules

    rules = load_rules()
    counts = {skin: 0 for skin in rules["skins"]}
    for line in golden:
        assert line["skin"] in counts, f"{line['skin']} is not one of the skins"
        counts[line["skin"]] += 1
    thin = {skin: count for skin, count in counts.items() if count < MIN_PER_SKIN}
    assert not thin, f"under {MIN_PER_SKIN} lines: {thin}"


def test_golden_voices_are_the_closed_set(golden: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
    used = {line["voice"] for line in golden}
    assert used <= VOICES
    assert used == VOICES


def test_golden_lines_are_all_different(golden: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
    prompts = [line["prompt"] for line in golden]
    assert len(set(prompts)) == len(prompts)


def test_golden_set_holds_no_demo_prompt(golden: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
    demo = {prompt.strip().lower() for prompt in DEMO_PROMPTS}
    clashes = [line["prompt"] for line in golden if line["prompt"].strip().lower() in demo]
    assert not clashes


def test_every_explicit_token_is_exercised_twice(golden: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
    from lib.rules import load_rules

    explicit = load_rules()["explicit"]
    for token in explicit["trailing_tokens"]:
        hits = [line for line in golden if line["prompt"].split()[-1].lower() == token]
        assert len(hits) >= 2, f"{token} appears {len(hits)} times as a trailing token"
        for line in hits:
            assert line["skin"] == explicit["trailing_tokens"][token]
    for word in explicit["whole_prompt"]:
        hits = [line for line in golden if word in line["prompt"].lower()]
        assert len(hits) >= 2, f"the trigger word {word} appears in {len(hits)} lines"


def test_golden_set_holds_enough_questions_that_are_not_decisions(golden: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
    questions = [
        line
        for line in golden
        if (line["prompt"].rstrip().endswith("?") or line["prompt"].split()[0].lower() in QUESTION_WORDS)
        and " or " not in line["prompt"].lower()
    ]
    assert len(questions) >= MIN_QUESTIONS


def test_golden_set_holds_enough_rants(golden: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
    rants = [
        line
        for line in golden
        if line["skin"] == "contract" and len(line["prompt"].replace(" and ", ", ").split(",")) >= 3
    ]
    assert len(rants) >= MIN_RANTS
    for rant in rants:
        clauses = len(rant["prompt"].replace(" and ", ", ").split(","))
        assert 3 <= clauses <= 8, f"{clauses} clauses: {rant['prompt']}"


def test_golden_set_holds_enough_insults_and_acks(golden: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
    banter = [line for line in golden if line["skin"] == "banter"]
    assert len(banter) >= MIN_BANTER
    acks = [
        line
        for line in golden
        if line["skin"] == "minimal" and not line["prompt"].split()[-1].startswith("/")
    ]
    assert len(acks) >= MIN_ACKS


def test_golden_set_holds_ambiguous_lines_that_fall_to_the_default(golden: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
    quiet = [
        line
        for line in golden
        if line["skin"] == "bluf"
        and not line["prompt"].rstrip().endswith("?")
        and not line["prompt"].split()[-1].startswith("/")
    ]
    assert len(quiet) >= MIN_AMBIGUOUS


def test_ledger_state_is_only_claimed_where_it_makes_sense(golden: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
    for line in golden:
        state = line.get("state") or {}
        if state.get("build_in_flight") or state.get("ledger_approved"):
            assert state.get("ledger_on_disk"), f"state without a ledger: {line['prompt']}"


def _algorithm_is_on_disk() -> bool:
    """True when ``lib.signals`` and ``score.decide`` are importable."""
    if importlib.util.find_spec("lib.signals") is None:
        return False
    from lib import score

    return hasattr(score, "decide") and hasattr(score, "score_skins")


@pytest.mark.skipif(
    not _algorithm_is_on_disk(),
    reason="lib.signals and score.decide are not importable",
)
def test_the_real_algorithm_runs_over_the_real_golden_set(golden: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
    from lib.rules import load_rules

    result = report.evaluate(golden, load_rules())
    assert isinstance(result.accuracy, float)
    assert 0.0 <= result.accuracy <= 1.0
    assert result.total == len(golden)
    assert sum(sum(row.values()) for row in result.confusion.values()) == len(golden)
    assert len(result.misses) == result.total - result.correct



================================================
FILE: tests/test_restore.py
================================================
"""The session-restore hook driven the way Claude Code drives it: a real process, JSON on stdin.

The question every test here asks is what a member sees in a window that has just been cleared: the
contract number and title on the first line, how the rows stand, and the rows themselves. The other
half is the silence: no contract, a struck row, a corrupt file, a child process, a reader that falls
over. A SessionStart hook that says the wrong thing is worse than one that says nothing, so each of
those has its own test rather than one shared assertion.

The ledger is built through ``lib.ledger`` itself, never by hand-writing JSON, so these tests break
if the on-disk shape changes underneath them.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path
from types import ModuleType
from typing import Any, Callable

import pytest

WINDOWS_CWD = "C:\\Users\\x"  # why: the payload field that carries backslashes on the member's OS
CONTRACT_ID = "0042"  # why: the number the frames print, so the head line reads as the walk shows it
TITLE = "members area"
SESSION = "s2"  # why: the cleared window is a new session id on the same contract, by design

# why: the frame-3 contract, one row per requirement the member said out loud
ROWS: list[tuple[str, str, str, str]] = [
    ("R1", "works on mobile", "pytest tests/test_mobile.py -q", ""),
    ("R2", "email-only login", "pytest tests/test_auth.py -q", ""),
    ("R3", "$47 everywhere", "grep -rn 29 src", ""),
    ("R4", "checkout untouched", "git diff --stat -- checkout/", ""),
    ("R5", "skool opens a new tab", "grep -rn target=_blank src", ""),
    ("R6", "feels premium", "python -c \"print(1)\"", ""),
    ("R7", "ship it by friday", "T-2d", ""),
]
PROVEN = ("R1", "R2", "R3", "R4", "R5")  # why: five rows the gate has already stamped ●

# why: the exact first line of the frame-5 restore, counts in the ledger's own reading order
EXPECTED_HEAD = "[router] restored contract #0042 · members area · 5 ● · 1 ○ · 1 ◐"
CARRY_ON = "The contract above is live; carry on from it."


def payload(source: str = "clear", session_id: str = SESSION) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """The SessionStart payload, as the harness sends it after a /clear."""
    return {"session_id": session_id, "source": source, "cwd": WINDOWS_CWD}


def context(stdout: str) -> str:
    """The additional context out of the one JSON object the hook wrote."""
    envelope = json.loads(stdout)
    assert envelope["hookSpecificOutput"]["hookEventName"] == "SessionStart"
    return envelope["hookSpecificOutput"]["additionalContext"]


def build_ledger() -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Write the frame-5 contract to this test's state folder: 5 ● proven, 1 ○ open, 1 ◐ on track."""
    from lib import ledger
    from lib.rules import load_rules

    prefixes = load_rules()["proofs"]["allow_prefixes"]
    ledger.from_table_rows(ROWS, TITLE, "s1", prefixes, contract_id=CONTRACT_ID)
    for row_id in PROVEN:
        ledger.stamp(row_id, "●", "1 passed")
    written = ledger.load()
    assert written is not None
    return written


def test_the_contract_is_built_the_way_the_frame_reads_it() -> None:
    """The fixture itself is asserted once, so a wrong count later is never a mystery."""
    from lib import ledger

    build_ledger()
    summary = ledger.summary()

    assert summary is not None
    assert summary["contract_id"] == CONTRACT_ID
    assert summary["total"] == len(ROWS)
    assert summary["proven"] == len(PROVEN)
    assert [row["status"] for row in ledger.load()["rows"]] == ["●"] * 5 + ["○", "◐"]


def test_a_cleared_session_gets_the_contract_back(run_hook: Callable[..., Any]) -> None:
    build_ledger()

    done = run_hook("restore", payload())

    assert done.returncode == 0
    assert done.stderr == ""
    injected = context(done.stdout)
    assert injected.startswith(EXPECTED_HEAD)
    assert CARRY_ON in injected


def test_every_row_comes_back_with_it(run_hook: Callable[..., Any]) -> None:
    build_ledger()

    injected = context(run_hook("restore", payload()).stdout)

    assert " R1 ● works on mobile · proof: pytest tests/test_mobile.py -q" in injected
    assert " R6 ○ feels premium · proof: python -c \"print(1)\"" in injected
    assert " R7 ◐ ship it by friday · proof: T-2d" in injected


def test_the_whole_reply_is_one_json_object(run_hook: Callable[..., Any]) -> None:
    build_ledger()

    done = run_hook("restore", payload())

    assert isinstance(json.loads(done.stdout), dict)
    assert done.stdout.count("hookSpecificOutput") == 1


def test_no_contract_means_no_restore(run_hook: Callable[..., Any]) -> None:
    done = run_hook("restore", payload(source="startup"))

    assert done.returncode == 0
    assert done.stdout == ""


def test_a_child_process_stands_down(run_hook: Callable[..., Any]) -> None:
    build_ledger()

    done = run_hook("restore", payload(), env={"PROMPT_ROUTER_CHILD": "1"})

    assert done.returncode == 0
    assert done.stdout == ""


def test_a_corrupt_contract_is_never_restored(
    run_hook: Callable[..., Any], state_dir: Path
) -> None:
    state_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    (state_dir / "ledger.json").write_text("{not json at all", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n")

    done = run_hook("restore", payload())

    assert done.returncode == 0
    assert done.stdout == ""
    # why: the member's bytes are kept, which is the ledger's own law, and the window opens clean
    assert list(state_dir.glob("ledger.bad.*.json"))


def test_a_struck_row_is_neither_counted_nor_owed(run_hook: Callable[..., Any]) -> None:
    from lib import ledger

    build_ledger()
    assert ledger.apply_edit("strike R6") == "R6 struck"

    injected = context(run_hook("restore", payload()).stdout)

    assert injected.startswith("[router] restored contract #0042 · members area · 5 ● · 1 ◐")
    assert " R6 · struck · feels premium" in injected


def test_a_reader_that_falls_over_stays_silent_and_is_logged(
    restore_hook: ModuleType,
    call_hook_inprocess: Callable[..., tuple[str, int]],
    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    state_dir: Path,
) -> None:
    from lib import ledger

    build_ledger()

    def fall_over(*args: object, **kwargs: object) -> str:
        raise RuntimeError("the view fell over")

    monkeypatch.setattr(ledger, "view", fall_over)

    out, code = call_hook_inprocess(restore_hook, payload(), capsys, monkeypatch)

    assert out == ""
    assert code == 0
    logged = (state_dir / "hooks.log").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    assert "Traceback" in logged
    assert "the view fell over" in logged



================================================
FILE: tests/test_router.py

"""The scored middle of the algorithm: threshold, tie band, and the explicit / house / rules order.

Every test drives ``decide`` against the real ``algorithm/rules.json`` with a hand-built signal
set, so the numbers here are the shipped weights. Handing the signals in also keeps this file
independent of ``lib/signals.py``: what each signal reads from a prompt is proven where it is
built, and what the weights do with those values is proven here.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path
from types import SimpleNamespace
from typing import Any

import pytest

from lib import hookio
from lib import rules as rules_module
from lib.score import decide, margin, score_skins

WS_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]  # why: tests/ sits directly under the workspace root
RULES_FILE = WS_ROOT / "algorithm" / "rules.json"  # why: the shipped algorithm, not a fixture copy

# why: decide() only reads the state when it has to compute the signals itself, and every test here
# hands them in, so this stand-in stays valid whatever lib/signals.State grows into.
NO_STATE: Any = SimpleNamespace(
    ledger_on_disk=False, ledger_approved=False, build_in_flight=False
)

# why: sitrep's three signals all firing, which is the shape of "where are we on the members thing"
SITREP_SIGNALS = {"progress_shape": 1.0, "ledger_on_disk": 1.0, "build_in_flight": 1.0}

# why: board 0.60 + 0.20 against sitrep 0.60 + 0.15, five hundredths apart inside a tie band of 0.10
TIE_SIGNALS = {"board_lexicon": 1.0, "ledger_on_disk": 1.0, "progress_shape": 1.0}

# why: bluf's question_shape alone pays 0.25, under the 0.35 threshold, so nothing clears
QUIET_SIGNALS = {"question_shape": 1.0}


@pytest.fixture
def rules(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """The shipped rules, validated against a stand-in skins folder.

    ``rules.py`` only checks that a file exists per skin, and the skin files are
    work, so one empty file per name validates the algorithm without waiting on them. What the
    files contain is tested where they are built.
    """
    names = json.loads(RULES_FILE.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))["skins"]
    stub = tmp_path / "skins"
    stub.mkdir()
    for name in names:
        (stub / f"{name}.md").write_text("", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n")
    monkeypatch.setattr(rules_module, "_skins_dir", lambda: stub)
    return rules_module.load_rules(RULES_FILE)


def test_a_clear_signal_set_routes_to_that_skin(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    decision = decide(
        "where are we on the members thing", NO_STATE, rules, signals=SITREP_SIGNALS
    )

    assert decision.skin == "sitrep"
    assert decision.source == "rules"
    assert decision.tier == "E3"
    assert decision.tie_with is None

    names = [name for name, _ in decision.signals_top3]
    amounts = [amount for _, amount in decision.signals_top3]
    assert names == ["progress_shape", "ledger_on_disk", "build_in_flight"]
    assert amounts == [pytest.approx(0.60), pytest.approx(0.15), pytest.approx(0.15)]
    assert amounts == sorted(amounts, reverse=True)
    assert decision.score == round(sum(amounts), 2) == pytest.approx(0.90)


def test_two_skins_inside_the_tie_band_carry_the_runner_up(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    scored = score_skins("how is the board looking", NO_STATE, rules, TIE_SIGNALS)
    decision = decide("how is the board looking", NO_STATE, rules, signals=TIE_SIGNALS)

    assert scored["board"][0] - scored["sitrep"][0] <= rules["tie_band"]
    assert decision.skin == "board"
    assert decision.tie_with == "sitrep"
    assert decision.source == "rules"
    assert decision.score == pytest.approx(0.80)


def test_the_signals_line_is_ordered_by_what_moved_the_score(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    # contract writes its signals as obligation_clauses, date_mention, campaign_lexicon, and these
    # values pay 0.45, 0.10 and 0.20, so the line only reads right if it is sorted by contribution
    signals = {"obligation_clauses": 3.0, "date_mention": 1.0, "campaign_lexicon": 1.0}
    decision = decide("three things by friday", NO_STATE, rules, signals=signals)

    assert decision.skin == "contract"
    assert decision.tier == "E4"
    assert decision.signals_top3 == [
        ("obligation_clauses", pytest.approx(0.45)),
        ("campaign_lexicon", pytest.approx(0.20)),
        ("date_mention", pytest.approx(0.10)),
    ]
    assert decision.score == pytest.approx(0.75)


def test_two_skins_on_the_same_total_route_the_same_way_every_time(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    # board and sitrep both land on 0.60 here; the winner is settled by name, so it never flips
    signals = {"board_lexicon": 1.0, "progress_shape": 1.0}
    first = decide("the board, and where are we", NO_STATE, rules, signals=signals)
    second = decide("the board, and where are we", NO_STATE, rules, signals=signals)

    assert (first.skin, first.tie_with) == ("board", "sitrep")
    assert (second.skin, second.tie_with) == ("board", "sitrep")


def test_nothing_above_the_threshold_falls_to_the_default(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    decision = decide("what time is it", NO_STATE, rules, signals=QUIET_SIGNALS)

    assert decision.skin == "bluf"
    assert decision.source == "default"
    assert decision.score == 0.0
    assert decision.tie_with is None
    # the best skin's signals ride along, so the injection can show why nothing cleared
    assert decision.signals_top3 == [("question_shape", pytest.approx(0.25))]


def test_the_house_floor_answers_when_nothing_clears(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    decision = decide("what time is it", NO_STATE, rules, "sitrep", signals=QUIET_SIGNALS)

    assert decision.skin == "sitrep"
    assert decision.source == "house"
    assert decision.tier == "E3"
    assert decision.score == 0.0


def test_an_explicit_token_beats_a_strong_signal_set(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    decision = decide(
        "where are we on the members thing /minimal", NO_STATE, rules, signals=SITREP_SIGNALS
    )

    assert decision.skin == "minimal"
    assert decision.source == "explicit"
    assert decision.score == 1.0
    assert decision.signals_top3 == []


def test_the_house_floor_never_overrides_a_rules_winner(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    decision = decide(
        "where are we on the members thing", NO_STATE, rules, "board", signals=SITREP_SIGNALS
    )

    assert decision.skin == "sitrep"
    assert decision.source == "rules"
    assert decision.score == pytest.approx(0.90)


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("clause_count", "expected"),
    [
        (2.0, 0.0),  # under the min of 3: two clauses are a sentence, not a list of requirements
        (3.0, 0.45),  # three clauses at 0.15 each
        (7.0, 0.75),  # seven clauses would pay 1.05, held at the cap
    ],
)
def test_a_per_clause_weight_pays_from_its_minimum_up_to_its_cap(
    rules: dict[str, Any], clause_count: float, expected: float
) -> None:
    scored = score_skins("anything", NO_STATE, rules, {"obligation_clauses": clause_count})
    total, contributions = scored["contract"]

    assert total == pytest.approx(expected)
    if expected:
        assert contributions == [("obligation_clauses", pytest.approx(expected))]
    else:
        assert contributions == []


def test_margin_is_the_gap_to_the_next_skin(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    # sitrep 0.90 against board and receipt on 0.20, which ledger_on_disk also feeds
    assert margin("where are we", NO_STATE, rules, signals=SITREP_SIGNALS) == pytest.approx(0.70)
    # board 0.80 against sitrep 0.75, the tie case: a narrow margin is the point of the tie band
    tie_gap = margin("how is the board looking", NO_STATE, rules, signals=TIE_SIGNALS)
    assert tie_gap == pytest.approx(0.05)
    # banter 0.90 with no other skin scoring at all, so the runner-up is 0.0
    alone = margin("you are useless", NO_STATE, rules, signals={"insult_at_model": 1.0})
    assert alone == pytest.approx(0.90)


def test_margin_of_an_explicit_prompt_is_certain(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    assert margin("go", NO_STATE, rules) == 1.0
    assert margin("anything at all /sitrep", NO_STATE, rules) == 1.0


def test_the_house_floor_persists_across_two_decisions(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    house_file = hookio.state_dir() / "house.json"
    hookio.atomic_write_json(house_file, {"skin": "sitrep"})

    seen = []
    for _ in range(2):
        house_skin = hookio.read_json(house_file, {}).get("skin")
        decision = decide("what time is it", NO_STATE, rules, house_skin, signals={})
        seen.append((decision.skin, decision.source))

    assert seen == [("sitrep", "house"), ("sitrep", "house")]

FILE: tests/test_rules.py

"""The shipped algorithm loads, and every bad value names its own key."""

from __future__ import annotations

import copy
import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import pytest

WS = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]  # why: tests/ sits one level under the repo root
RULES_JSON = WS / "algorithm" / "rules.json"
REAL_SKINS_DIR = WS / "skins"
SHIPPED_SKINS = (
    "minimal",
    "bluf",
    "sitrep",
    "contract",
    "bars",
    "board",
    "receipt",
    "banter",
)  # why: the eight skins the plan ships; rules.json is expected to name exactly these

from lib import rules as rules_mod


def _shipped() -> dict[str, Any]:
    return json.loads(RULES_JSON.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))


def _fake_skins(tmp_path: Path, names: tuple[str, ...] = SHIPPED_SKINS) -> Path:
    """A skins folder holding empty files, so validation can check names without the real skins."""
    folder = tmp_path / "skins"
    folder.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    for name in names:
        with open(folder / f"{name}.md", "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as handle:
            handle.write("")
    return folder


def _write_rules(tmp_path: Path, data: dict[str, Any]) -> Path:
    path = tmp_path / "rules.json"
    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as handle:
        handle.write(json.dumps(data, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
    return path


def _point_at(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch, folder: Path) -> None:
    monkeypatch.setattr(rules_mod, "_skins_dir", lambda: folder)


def test_shipped_rules_load_against_a_stand_in_skins_folder(
    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
) -> None:
    _point_at(monkeypatch, _fake_skins(tmp_path))
    loaded = rules_mod.load_rules(RULES_JSON)
    assert tuple(loaded["skins"]) == SHIPPED_SKINS
    assert loaded["classifier"] == "rules"
    assert loaded["default_skin"] == "bluf"
    assert loaded["threshold"] == 0.35
    assert loaded["tie_band"] == 0.10
    assert loaded["explicit"]["trailing_tokens"]["/e5"] == "contract"
    assert loaded["explicit"]["whole_prompt"]["go"] == "bars"
    assert "pytest" in loaded["proofs"]["allow_prefixes"]


# runs against the real skins folder
@pytest.mark.skipif(
    not all((REAL_SKINS_DIR / f"{name}.md").is_file() for name in SHIPPED_SKINS),
    reason="skins/*.md are missing",
)
def test_shipped_rules_load_against_the_real_skins_folder() -> None:
    loaded = rules_mod.load_rules()
    assert tuple(loaded["skins"]) == SHIPPED_SKINS
    assert rules_mod.rules_path() == RULES_JSON


def test_a_missing_skin_file_names_that_skin(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
    folder = _fake_skins(tmp_path)
    (folder / "board.md").unlink()
    _point_at(monkeypatch, folder)
    with pytest.raises(rules_mod.RulesError) as caught:
        rules_mod.load_rules(RULES_JSON)
    assert "board" in str(caught.value)


def test_an_unknown_classifier_names_classifier(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
    _point_at(monkeypatch, _fake_skins(tmp_path))
    data = _shipped()
    data["classifier"] = "gpt"
    with pytest.raises(rules_mod.RulesError) as caught:
        rules_mod.load_rules(_write_rules(tmp_path, data))
    assert "classifier" in str(caught.value)


def test_a_threshold_out_of_range_names_threshold(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
    _point_at(monkeypatch, _fake_skins(tmp_path))
    data = _shipped()
    data["threshold"] = 1.5
    with pytest.raises(rules_mod.RulesError) as caught:
        rules_mod.load_rules(_write_rules(tmp_path, data))
    assert "threshold" in str(caught.value)


def test_a_trailing_token_pointing_nowhere_names_the_token(
    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
) -> None:
    _point_at(monkeypatch, _fake_skins(tmp_path))
    data = _shipped()
    data["explicit"]["trailing_tokens"]["/zzz"] = "nope"
    with pytest.raises(rules_mod.RulesError) as caught:
        rules_mod.load_rules(_write_rules(tmp_path, data))
    assert "/zzz" in str(caught.value)


def test_the_fallback_passes_the_same_validator(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
    _point_at(monkeypatch, _fake_skins(tmp_path, ("bluf",)))
    before = copy.deepcopy(rules_mod.FALLBACK_RULES)
    validated = rules_mod.validate_rules(rules_mod.FALLBACK_RULES)
    assert validated is rules_mod.FALLBACK_RULES
    assert rules_mod.FALLBACK_RULES == before
    assert validated["default_skin"] in validated["skins"]

FILE: tests/test_score.py

"""The two certain edges of the algorithm, driven with the shipped rules.json.

Everything here routes against the real ``algorithm/rules.json``, so a token that stops working in
the shipped file fails a test rather than a member's session.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import pytest

from lib import rules as rules_module
from lib.score import default_decision, explicit_decision, tier_of

WS_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]  # why: tests/ sits directly under the workspace root
RULES_FILE = WS_ROOT / "algorithm" / "rules.json"  # why: the shipped algorithm, not a fixture copy


@pytest.fixture
def rules(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """The shipped rules, validated against a stand-in skins folder.

    ``rules.py`` only checks that a file exists per skin, and the skin files themselves are another
    own tests, so one empty file per name is enough to validate here and keeps these tests
    independent of them. What the files contain is tested where they are built.
    """
    names = json.loads(RULES_FILE.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))["skins"]
    stub = tmp_path / "skins"
    stub.mkdir()
    for name in names:
        (stub / f"{name}.md").write_text("", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n")
    monkeypatch.setattr(rules_module, "_skins_dir", lambda: stub)
    return rules_module.load_rules(RULES_FILE)


def test_trailing_token_wins_and_carries_the_skins_tier(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    decision = explicit_decision("anything /sitrep", rules)
    assert decision is not None
    assert decision.skin == "sitrep"
    assert decision.tier == "E3"
    assert decision.source == "explicit"
    assert decision.score == 1.0
    assert decision.signals_top3 == []
    assert decision.tie_with is None


def test_tier_token_names_its_own_tier(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    decision = explicit_decision("what time is it /e5", rules)
    assert decision is not None
    assert decision.skin == "contract"
    assert decision.tier == "E5"  # the token's number, not the contract skin's own E4
    assert decision.source == "explicit"


def test_tier_token_on_its_own_is_still_explicit(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    decision = explicit_decision("/e5", rules)
    assert decision is not None
    assert decision.skin == "contract"
    assert decision.tier == "E5"
    assert decision.source == "explicit"


def test_whole_word_prompt_routes(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    decision = explicit_decision("sitrep", rules)
    assert decision is not None
    assert decision.skin == "sitrep"
    assert decision.source == "explicit"


def test_go_is_the_whole_prompt_form_of_bars(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    decision = explicit_decision("go", rules)
    assert decision is not None
    assert decision.skin == "bars"
    assert decision.tier == "E4"


def test_a_whole_word_inside_a_sentence_is_not_explicit(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    # "go" ends the prompt but is a whole-prompt word, not a trailing token, so it decides nothing
    assert explicit_decision("should we go", rules) is None


def test_case_and_surrounding_space_do_not_matter(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    decision = explicit_decision("  ANYTHING /SITREP  ", rules)
    assert decision is not None
    assert decision.skin == "sitrep"


def test_a_plain_prompt_names_nothing(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    assert explicit_decision("what is the capital of peru", rules) is None
    assert explicit_decision("   ", rules) is None


def test_default_decision_is_the_rules_default(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    decision = default_decision(rules)
    assert decision.skin == "bluf"
    assert decision.tier == "E2"
    assert decision.source == "default"
    assert decision.score == 0.0


def test_house_floor_replaces_the_default(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    decision = default_decision(rules, "sitrep")
    assert decision.skin == "sitrep"
    assert decision.tier == "E3"
    assert decision.source == "house"
    assert decision.score == 0.0


def test_tier_of_names_the_unknown(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    assert tier_of("banter", rules) == "E1"
    assert tier_of("not-a-skin", rules) == "E?"

FILE: tests/test_settings.py

"""The wiring tests: `.claude/settings.json` and the Router output style.

These read the two files as a cloner's Claude Code reads them, so a rename, a
retyped command or a dropped timeout fails here before it fails in a session.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

# why: tests/ sits one level under the repo root, so parents[1] is the workspace root
WS: Path = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
SETTINGS_PATH: Path = WS / ".claude" / "settings.json"
STYLE_PATH: Path = WS / ".claude" / "output-styles" / "router.md"
HOOKS_DIR: Path = WS / ".claude" / "hooks"

# why: the three events the plan wires, each to its own hook file and timeout
# (router 10 s, restore 10 s, gate 60 s because the proofs run inside it)
EXPECTED_HOOKS: dict[str, tuple[str, int]] = {
    "UserPromptSubmit": ("router.py", 10),
    "Stop": ("gate.py", 60),
    "SessionStart": ("restore.py", 10),
}

# why: the literal Claude Code expands to the project root; a hard-coded path breaks every cloner
PROJECT_DIR_TOKEN: str = "${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}"

# why: the matcher the plan pins, so /clear and /compact both restore the ledger
SESSION_START_MATCHER: str = "startup|resume|clear|compact"

# why: the style is appended to the system prompt every turn, so it stays small
MAX_STYLE_BODY_LINES: int = 25


def load_settings() -> dict:
    """The settings file as JSON. A parse error fails the test that calls this."""
    return json.loads(SETTINGS_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))


def parse_front_matter(path: Path) -> tuple[dict[str, str], str]:
    """Split `key: value` front matter from the body, by hand, no yaml dependency.

    Returns the keys and the body with leading and trailing blank lines stripped.
    """
    text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    lines = text.splitlines()
    assert lines and lines[0].strip() == "---", f"{path.name}: no front matter opener"
    keys: dict[str, str] = {}
    body_start = len(lines)
    for index, line in enumerate(lines[1:], start=1):
        if line.strip() == "---":
            body_start = index + 1
            break
        if ":" in line:
            key, _, value = line.partition(":")
            keys[key.strip()] = value.strip()
    else:  # pragma: no cover - only reachable on a malformed file
        pytest.fail(f"{path.name}: front matter never closes")
    return keys, "\n".join(lines[body_start:]).strip("\n").strip()


def single_hook(settings: dict, event: str) -> dict:
    """The one command hook wired to `event`."""
    groups = settings["hooks"][event]
    assert isinstance(groups, list) and len(groups) == 1, f"{event}: expected one hook group"
    hooks = groups[0]["hooks"]
    assert isinstance(hooks, list) and len(hooks) == 1, f"{event}: expected one hook"
    return hooks[0]


def test_settings_file_is_valid_json() -> None:
    assert SETTINGS_PATH.is_file(), f"missing {SETTINGS_PATH}"
    settings = load_settings()
    assert isinstance(settings, dict)


def test_output_style_is_router() -> None:
    assert load_settings()["outputStyle"] == "Router"


def test_output_style_matches_the_style_front_matter_name() -> None:
    keys, _ = parse_front_matter(STYLE_PATH)
    assert keys["name"] == load_settings()["outputStyle"], (
        "outputStyle must match the style's front matter name, not its filename"
    )


def test_style_front_matter_carries_the_three_keys() -> None:
    keys, _ = parse_front_matter(STYLE_PATH)
    assert keys["description"], "the style needs a description line"
    assert keys["keep-coding-instructions"] == "true"


def test_hooks_declare_exactly_the_three_events() -> None:
    assert set(load_settings()["hooks"]) == set(EXPECTED_HOOKS)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("event", sorted(EXPECTED_HOOKS))
def test_each_hook_is_one_python_command_on_its_own_file(event: str) -> None:
    filename, _ = EXPECTED_HOOKS[event]
    hook = single_hook(load_settings(), event)
    command = hook["command"]
    assert hook["type"] == "command"
    assert PROJECT_DIR_TOKEN in command, f"{event}: command must use {PROJECT_DIR_TOKEN}"
    assert command.startswith("python "), f"{event}: bare python launcher, no uv, no shell key"
    assert "shell" not in hook, f"{event}: the shell key is deliberately dropped"
    assert command.endswith(f'/.claude/hooks/{filename}"'), (
        f"{event}: the path must end with the hook filename in double quotes"
    )


@pytest.mark.parametrize("event", sorted(EXPECTED_HOOKS))
def test_each_hook_carries_its_timeout(event: str) -> None:
    _, timeout = EXPECTED_HOOKS[event]
    assert single_hook(load_settings(), event)["timeout"] == timeout


def test_session_start_matcher() -> None:
    group = load_settings()["hooks"]["SessionStart"][0]
    assert group["matcher"] == SESSION_START_MATCHER

    assert (HOOKS_DIR / "router.py").is_file(), "the UserPromptSubmit hook file is missing"

    assert (HOOKS_DIR / "restore.py").is_file(), "the SessionStart hook file is missing"

"""Every signal, one at a time, against the lexicons the repo actually ships.

Three prompts that should fire it, three that should not, and one edge that says out loud what the
signal does at its boundary. The rules are loaded from ``algorithm/rules.json`` rather than a
fixture, so a word list changed in the shipped file shows up here as a failing sentence.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import pytest

from lib import hookio, rules as rules_module, signals
from lib.signals import State

RULES: dict[str, Any] = rules_module.load_rules()
LEXICONS: dict[str, Any] = RULES["lexicons"]
NO_STATE = State()

# why: frame 3 of the walk, typed the way the member typed it; the pinned count of 7 comes from here
RANT = (
    "ok the members area needs to work on mobile, email-only login, "
    "$47 everywhere no $29 left, don't touch checkout, skool opens a new tab, "
    "feels premium, due friday"
)

# why: frame 1 of the walk, the decision the router has to read as a decision
FRAME_1 = "should I post the graph reel on tiktok too or keep it on instagram"

# why: frame 2 of the walk, the progress ask
FRAME_2 = "where are we on the members area"


def _write_ledger(rows: list[dict[str, str]], approved: bool) -> Path:
    """Put a ledger on disk in this test's state folder and hand back its path."""
    payload: dict[str, Any] = {"contract_id": "0042", "title": "members area", "rows": rows}
    if approved:
        payload["approved_at"] = "2026-08-18T20:14:00"
    path = hookio.state_dir() / "ledger.json"
    path.write_text(json.dumps(payload), encoding="utf-8", newline="\n")
    return path

    """A number on its own is a mark out of ten with the ten left off."""
    assert signals.ack_or_rating("7", NO_STATE, LEXICONS) == 1.0

    assert signals.very_short("", NO_STATE, LEXICONS) == 0.0

def test_ledger_on_disk_edge_ignores_the_prompt() -> None:
    """A state signal reads the disk, never the wording."""
    state = State(ledger_on_disk=True)
    assert signals.ledger_on_disk("", state, LEXICONS) == signals.ledger_on_disk(RANT, state, LEXICONS)

    assert signals.ledger_approved("go", state, LEXICONS) == 1.0

)
def test_ledger_approved_negatives(state: State) -> None:
    assert signals.ledger_approved("go", state, LEXICONS) == 0.0


def test_ledger_approved_edge_a_ledger_alone_is_not_approval() -> None:
    """A contract on disk that nobody said go to scores nothing here."""
    assert signals.ledger_approved("go", State(ledger_on_disk=True), LEXICONS) == 0.0


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "state",
    [
        State(build_in_flight=True),
