    ("build it", "bars", 0.9, "the go word, the build starts now"),
    ("thanks", "minimal", 0.92, "an acknowledgement, one line back"),
    ("show me the board", "board", 0.87, "asks for the requirements as columns"),
    ("give me the bill", "receipt", 0.85, "asks for the closed contract printed"),
    ("you're hopeless", "banter", 0.9, "an insult aimed at the model"),
)

_INTRO = (
    "You are the skin classifier for a prompt router. Read the member's latest message and name "
    "the one skin that should shape the reply."
)
_SKINS_HEADING = "Skins:"
_CONTRACT_HEADING = "Answer with one JSON object and nothing else:"
_EXAMPLES_HEADING = "Examples:"
_CLOSER = (
    "Pick exactly one skin from that list. No fence, no prose, nothing outside the JSON object."
)


def _use_line(name: str) -> str:
    """The skin's own ``use`` line, or the empty string when its file cannot be read."""
    try:
        return skins.load_skin(name).use
    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — a broken skin file costs a description, not the call
        hookio.log(f"haiku: no use line for skin {name}: {exc}")
        return ""


def classifier_prompt(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """The system prompt: the skins with their use lines, the JSON contract, eight examples."""
    names = [name for name in rules.get("skins", {})]
    lines = [_INTRO, "", _SKINS_HEADING]
    for name in names:
        use = _use_line(name)
        lines.append(f"- {name}: {use}" if use else f"- {name}")

    choices = ", ".join(names)
    contract = (
        '{"skin": "<one of ' + choices + '>", '
        '"confidence": 0.0-1.0, "reason": "<one short line>"}'
    )
    lines += ["", _CONTRACT_HEADING, contract, "", _EXAMPLES_HEADING]
    for prompt, skin, confidence, reason in EXAMPLES:
        answer = json.dumps(
            {"skin": skin, "confidence": confidence, "reason": reason}, ensure_ascii=False
        )
        lines.append(f"{prompt} -> {answer}")
    lines += ["", _CLOSER]
    return "\n".join(lines)


def _child_env() -> dict[str, str]:
    """This process's environment, minus the variables a nested call must not inherit."""
    env = dict(os.environ)
    for name in SCRUBBED_ENV:
        env.pop(name, None)
    env[hookio.CHILD_ENV] = "1"
    return env


def _argv(claude_path: str, system_prompt: str) -> list[str]:
    """The headless command line, the resolved binary first so Windows runs it without a shell."""
    return [
        claude_path,
        "--print",
        "--model",
        MODEL_ALIAS,
        "--output-format",
        "json",
        "--tools",
        NO_TOOLS,
        "--max-turns",
        MAX_TURNS,
        "--setting-sources",
        SETTING_SOURCES,
        "--system-prompt",
        system_prompt,
    ]


def _strip_fence(text: str) -> str:
    """The JSON body without a code fence around it."""
    stripped = text.strip()
    match = _FENCE.match(stripped)
    return match.group("body").strip() if match else stripped


def _answer(stdout: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
    """The classifier's own object, dug out of the ``--output-format json`` envelope."""
    envelope = json.loads(stdout)
    if not isinstance(envelope, dict):
        hookio.log("haiku: the envelope was not an object")
        return None
    result = envelope.get("result")
    if isinstance(result, dict):
        return result
    if not isinstance(result, str) or not result.strip():
        hookio.log("haiku: the envelope carried no result text")
        return None
    answer = json.loads(_strip_fence(result))
    if not isinstance(answer, dict):
        hookio.log("haiku: the result text was not an object")
        return None
    return answer


def _confidence(value: Any) -> float:
    """The answer's confidence, clamped into the contract's range; 0.0 when it is not a number."""
    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, float)):
        return MIN_CONFIDENCE
    return round(min(MAX_CONFIDENCE, max(MIN_CONFIDENCE, float(value))), SCORE_DIGITS)


def classify(prompt: str, recent_context: str, rules: dict[str, Any]) -> Decision | None:
    """Ask Haiku which skin fits, or return ``None`` so the router scores the prompt itself.

    ``None`` covers every unhappy path: the child guard, no ``claude`` on PATH, a non-zero exit, a
    timeout, an answer that will not parse, and a skin the rules do not list. The reason is logged,
    never printed: this module runs inside a hook, where stdout belongs to the envelope alone.
    """
    try:
        if hookio.is_child_process():
            return None
        claude_path = shutil.which(CLAUDE_BINARY)
        if not claude_path:
            hookio.log("haiku: no claude on PATH, staying with the rules")
            return None

        payload = f"{recent_context}\n\n{prompt}" if recent_context.strip() else prompt
        completed = subprocess.run(  # noqa: S603 — a resolved absolute path, no shell, fixed argv
            _argv(claude_path, classifier_prompt(rules)),
            input=payload,
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
            encoding="utf-8",
            timeout=CLASSIFIER_TIMEOUT_S,
            env=_child_env(),
            shell=False,
            check=False,
        )
        if completed.returncode != 0:
            hookio.log(f"haiku: claude exited {completed.returncode}")
            return None

        answer = _answer(completed.stdout or "")
        if answer is None:
            return None

        skin = answer.get("skin")
        available = rules.get("skins")
        if not isinstance(skin, str) or not isinstance(available, dict) or skin not in available:
            hookio.log(f"haiku: {skin!r} is not one of the skins, staying with the rules")
            return None

        score = _confidence(answer.get("confidence"))
        return Decision(
            skin=skin,
            tier=tier_of(skin, rules),
            score=score,
            source=SOURCE,
            signals_top3=[(SOURCE, score)],
            tie_with=None,
        )
    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — the never-crash law: the rules answer instead
        hookio.log(f"haiku: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}")
        return None



================================================
FILE: .claude/hooks/lib/hookio.py
================================================
"""Hook plumbing: stdin repair, the stdout envelope, the byte budget, state files, logging.

Every hook in this repo talks to Claude Code through this module and nothing else. There is no
``print`` anywhere under ``.claude/hooks``: ``emit_context``, ``emit_block`` and ``emit_nothing``
are the only writers to stdout, so a stray line can never corrupt the JSON the harness reads.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import re
import sys
import tempfile
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

# why: the platform cap is 10,000 characters and an overflow becomes a file pointer the model may
# never follow; one character is at least one byte, so 7,500 bytes can never exceed that cap.
ROUTER_MAX_BYTES = 7_500

# why: Claude Code on Windows can pass a path with lone backslashes ("C:\\Users" spelled with one),
# which is invalid JSON; this matches a backslash that is neither already escaped nor escaping a
# quote or a backslash, so a correctly escaped payload is left alone.
_LONE_BACKSLASH = re.compile(r'(?<!\\)\\(?!["\\])')

# why: tests and the demo project point the state store at their own directory; read at call time
# so a test that changes it between two calls gets two different answers.
STATE_DIR_ENV = "PROMPT_ROUTER_STATE_DIR"

# why: the recursion marker; a hook that spawns Claude sets it so the child's own hooks stand down.
CHILD_ENV = "PROMPT_ROUTER_CHILD"

# why: one log file per state dir keeps the router, the gate and the restore hook on one timeline.
LOG_NAME = "hooks.log"

# why: Claude Code writes the payload as UTF-8 on every OS, so the bytes are decoded here rather
# than by sys.stdin, whose encoding on a Windows console is the codepage (cp1252) and would read
# "café" as "cafÃ©" before the JSON parser ever saw it.
STDIN_ENCODING = "utf-8"

# why: one stray byte costs one replacement character, never the member's whole turn.
STDIN_ERRORS = "replace"

# why: lib -> hooks -> .claude -> the repo root; four levels up from this file.
_ROOT_DEPTH = 3


def repo_root() -> Path:
    """The repository root: the folder holding ``.claude``, ``skins`` and ``algorithm``."""
    return Path(__file__).resolve().parents[_ROOT_DEPTH]


def state_dir() -> Path:
    """The router's state folder, created if missing.

    ``PROMPT_ROUTER_STATE_DIR`` wins when it is set, and it is read on every call: a test that
    repoints it between two calls gets the new path from the second one.
    """
    override = os.environ.get(STATE_DIR_ENV)
    path = Path(override) if override else repo_root() / ".router"
    path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    return path


def is_child_process() -> bool:
    """True when this process was spawned by one of our own hooks (the recursion guard)."""
    return bool(os.environ.get(CHILD_ENV))


def _stdin_text() -> str:
    """The payload as text, decoded UTF-8, whatever the console codepage says.

    The raw bytes come from ``sys.stdin.buffer`` so the accented characters a member types survive
    the trip. A stdin with no ``buffer`` is already text (a test putting a ``StringIO`` in place),
    so it is read as it stands.
    """
    buffer = getattr(sys.stdin, "buffer", None)
    if buffer is None:
        return sys.stdin.read()
    raw = buffer.read()
    if isinstance(raw, bytes):
        return raw.decode(STDIN_ENCODING, errors=STDIN_ERRORS)
    return str(raw)


def read_hook_input() -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Read the harness payload from stdin.

    The bytes are decoded UTF-8 first, then a normal parse; on a decode error the Windows
    lone-backslash repair, then one more parse. Anything still unreadable, or a payload that is not
    an object, returns ``{}`` and is logged: a hook that cannot read its input stays silent rather
    than guessing.
    """
    try:
        raw = _stdin_text()
    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — a closed or undecodable stdin is not a crash
        log(f"read_hook_input: stdin unreadable: {exc}")
        return {}

    if not raw.strip():
        return {}

    try:
        data: Any = json.loads(raw)
    except json.JSONDecodeError:
        try:
            data = json.loads(_LONE_BACKSLASH.sub(r"\\\\", raw))
        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
            log(f"read_hook_input: payload did not parse: {exc}")
            return {}

    if not isinstance(data, dict):
        log(f"read_hook_input: payload was {type(data).__name__}, not an object")
        return {}
    return data


def emit_context(event: str, text: str) -> None:
    """Write the additional-context envelope, once, with no trailing newline.

    ``json.dumps`` escapes non-ASCII by default, which is deliberate: the wire stays pure ASCII so
    a cp1252 console cannot mangle the block letters, and Claude Code decodes them back.
    """
    sys.stdout.write(
        json.dumps(
            {"hookSpecificOutput": {"hookEventName": event, "additionalContext": text}}
        )
    )
    sys.stdout.flush()


def emit_block(reason: str) -> None:
    """Write the block decision the Stop gate uses, once, with no trailing newline."""
    sys.stdout.write(json.dumps({"decision": "block", "reason": reason}))
    sys.stdout.flush()


def emit_nothing() -> None:
    """Say nothing at all: no stdout, no exit code change. The silent path."""
    return None


def clip_bytes(text: str, budget: int) -> str:
    """Clip ``text`` to at most ``budget`` UTF-8 bytes.

    Whole lines drop from the end first, so the top of the injection (the router line, then the
    header) always survives. A first line that alone exceeds the budget is cut on codepoint
    boundaries, so the result is always valid text.
    """
    if len(text.encode("utf-8")) <= budget:
        return text

    first, *rest = text.split("\n")
    kept = [first]
    used = len(first.encode("utf-8"))
    for line in rest:
        line_bytes = len(line.encode("utf-8")) + 1  # why: the newline that rejoins this line
        if used + line_bytes > budget:
            break
        kept.append(line)
        used += line_bytes

    clipped = "\n".join(kept)
    if len(clipped.encode("utf-8")) > budget:
        clipped = _clip_codepoints(clipped, budget)
    return clipped


def _clip_codepoints(text: str, budget: int) -> str:
    """Cut on codepoint boundaries, measured in encoded bytes. Never splits a character."""
    out: list[str] = []
    total = 0
    for char in text:
        size = len(char.encode("utf-8"))
        if total + size > budget:
            break
        out.append(char)
        total += size
    return "".join(out)


def atomic_write_json(path: Path, obj: Any) -> None:
    """Write JSON crash-atomically: a temp file beside the target, then ``os.replace``.

    A crash mid-write leaves the previous file whole. ``newline="\\n"`` keeps the file LF on every
    OS, so a state file written on Windows reads the same everywhere.
    """
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    text = json.dumps(obj, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)

    handle, tmp_name = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=str(path.parent), prefix=f"{path.name}.", suffix=".tmp")
    os.close(handle)
    tmp = Path(tmp_name)
    try:
        with open(tmp, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as stream:
            stream.write(text)
            stream.flush()
            os.fsync(stream.fileno())
        os.replace(tmp, path)
    except BaseException:
        tmp.unlink(missing_ok=True)
        raise


def read_json(path: Path, default: Any = None) -> Any:
    """Read JSON from ``path``; return ``default`` when it is missing, unreadable or not JSON."""
    try:
        with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as stream:
            return json.load(stream)
    except (OSError, ValueError):
        return default


def log(line: str) -> None:
    """Append one timestamped line to ``state_dir()/hooks.log``. Never raises.

    Logging is the last thing that may break a hook, so every failure here is swallowed: an
    unwritable state folder costs a log line, not the member's prompt.
    """
    try:
        target = state_dir() / LOG_NAME
        stamp = datetime.now().isoformat(timespec="seconds")
        with open(target, "a", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as stream:
            stream.write(f"{stamp} {line}\n")
    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — the never-crash law: a log failure is silent
        return None
    return None



================================================
FILE: .claude/hooks/lib/ledger.py
================================================
"""The contract ledger: what the member agreed to, what has been proven, and what changed.

One file on disk (``state_dir()/ledger.json``) holds the live contract, and one plain counter file
holds the last contract number. Everything the router, the gate and the restore hook show comes
from here, so three rules run through the whole module:

* a read never costs the turn. A missing ledger is ``None``, a corrupt one is moved aside to
  ``ledger.bad.<ts>.json`` with one log line, and the caller carries on without that section.
* the member's word outranks the model's. A struck row stays struck when the model re-prints the
  table; only the member's own edit brings it back.
* nothing is thrown away. Every strike, reword, reproof, approval and proof run appends to the
  row's ``history``, so the receipt at the end can say what happened rather than what is true now.

Row status glyphs: ``○`` open, ``●`` proven, ``✗`` failed, ``◐`` on track (a date row), ``?`` no
measurable, ``⏱`` timed out, ``manual`` a check a person makes.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import re
from datetime import datetime, timedelta
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, NamedTuple

from lib import hookio, proofs

LEDGER_NAME = "ledger.json"  # why: one file per repo holds the live contract; the gate rewrites it whole
COUNTER_NAME = "counter"  # why: plain text survives a hand edit better than a field inside the ledger
BAD_PREFIX = "ledger.bad."  # why: a corrupt ledger is kept, not deleted; the member may want to read it
FIRST_ID = "0001"  # why: the first contract in a fresh clone reads as #0001, never #0
ID_WIDTH = 4  # why: four digits keep every contract id the same width in the header and the injection
DEFAULT_MAX_ROWS = 7  # why: seven rows is the tallest contract table the frames hold inside 88 columns

OPEN_GLYPH = "○"  # why: a requirement that is agreed and not yet proven
PROVEN_GLYPH = "●"  # why: the proof ran and passed
FAILED_GLYPH = "✗"  # why: the proof ran and did not pass; the gate's catch
ON_TRACK_GLYPH = "◐"  # why: a date row is on track or it is not, so it never reads as proven
UNDEFINED_GLYPH = "?"  # why: the requirement has no measurable yet, and saying so beats inventing one
TIMEOUT_GLYPH = "⏱"  # why: the proof ran out of the gate's time budget, which is not a failure
MANUAL_STATUS = "manual"  # why: a check a person makes; it is never run and it never blocks

BUILT_GLYPHS = (PROVEN_GLYPH, ON_TRACK_GLYPH)  # why: built means the work exists; proven is the subset that passed

# why: the order and the words the SITREP-lite line reads out, spelled once so the frames and the
# restore hook cannot drift apart
COUNT_WORDS: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...] = (
    (PROVEN_GLYPH, "proven"),
    (OPEN_GLYPH, "open"),
    (ON_TRACK_GLYPH, "on track"),
    (UNDEFINED_GLYPH, "undefined"),
    (FAILED_GLYPH, "failed"),
    (TIMEOUT_GLYPH, "timed out"),
    (MANUAL_STATUS, "manual"),
)

KIND_AUTO = "auto"  # why: the proof is a command the gate may run for the member
KIND_MANUAL = "manual"  # why: the proof is a command we do not run, or a check a person makes
KIND_UNDEFINED = "undefined"  # why: there is no measurable yet, so there is nothing to run
KIND_DATE = "date"  # why: the row is a deadline; time proves it, not a command

ROW_INDENT = " "  # why: one leading space sets the rows under their heading in the injection
PROOF_SEPARATOR = " · proof: "  # why: the fixed wording the frames show between a requirement and its proof
STRUCK_SEPARATOR = " · struck · "  # why: a struck row still shows, marked, so the member sees their own edit
MORE_LINE = " … and {count} more"  # why: the clip says how much it kept back rather than ending mid-contract
CONTRACT_LINE = "contract #{contract_id}"  # why: the first data line of the SITREP-lite frame
LINE_JOIN = " · "  # why: the separator the frames use inside a data line

DUE_HOUR = 17  # why: end of the working day is the natural deadline when the member gives none
MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS = 400  # why: the ledger is read on every turn, so one proof's output cannot bloat it

EVENT_CREATED = "created"  # why: the things that happen to a row, spelled once for the history
EVENT_REWORDED = "reworded"
EVENT_REPROOFED = "reproofed"  # why: a new measure is its own change; the wording can stay as it was
EVENT_STRUCK = "struck"
EVENT_PROOF = "proof"
EVENT_APPROVED = "approved"

# why: the deadline words a requirement can carry; a weekday name is a date the member reads as one
WEEKDAYS = ("monday", "tuesday", "wednesday", "thursday", "friday", "saturday", "sunday")
_ISO_DATE = re.compile(r"\b(\d{4})-(\d{2})-(\d{2})\b")
_TMINUS_PROOF = re.compile(r"^t-\d+d")  # why: the proof the contract skin writes for a date row
_WEEKDAY_WORD = re.compile(r"\b(" + "|".join(WEEKDAYS) + r")\b")
_TODAY_WORDS = ("eod", "today", "end of day")  # why: three spellings of the same deadline
_TOMORROW = "tomorrow"

# why: the member edits the contract by number: "strike R3", "strike 3", "reword R3 ...", "R3: ...",
# and either wording spelling may carry a new proof after " · proof: "
_STRIKE = re.compile(r"^\s*strike\s+r?(\d+)\s*$", re.IGNORECASE)
_REWORD = re.compile(r"^\s*reword\s+r?(\d+)[\s:]+(.+?)\s*$", re.IGNORECASE | re.DOTALL)
_COLON = re.compile(r"^\s*r(\d+)\s*:\s*(.+?)\s*$", re.IGNORECASE | re.DOTALL)
# why: a row's measure can change on its own, so "R3 proof: pytest -q" names no new wording
_PROOF_ONLY = re.compile(
    r"^\s*r?(\d+)(?:\s+|\s*·\s*)proof\s*:\s*(.+?)\s*$", re.IGNORECASE | re.DOTALL
)
# why: the separator the ledger view itself prints, so a member can edit a line they read back
_PROOF_TAIL = re.compile(r"\s*·\s*proof\s*:\s*(.+?)\s*$", re.IGNORECASE | re.DOTALL)
_PROOF_HEAD = re.compile(r"^\s*proof\s*:\s*(.+?)\s*$", re.IGNORECASE | re.DOTALL)

_RULES_RELATIVE = ("algorithm", "rules.json")  # why: where the allowlist lives when a caller passes none


def ledger_path() -> Path:
    """Where the live contract sits."""
    return hookio.state_dir() / LEDGER_NAME


def counter_path() -> Path:
    """Where the contract number sits."""
    return hookio.state_dir() / COUNTER_NAME


def next_id() -> str:
    """The number the next contract would get. Reads only: calling this never burns an id."""
    try:
        raw = counter_path().read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip()
    except OSError:
        return FIRST_ID
    try:
        value = int(raw)
    except ValueError:
        hookio.log(f"ledger: the counter file does not hold a number ({raw!r}); reading it as none")
        return FIRST_ID
    return f"{value + 1:0{ID_WIDTH}d}"


def _write_counter(contract_id: str) -> None:
    """Move the counter to this contract's number. Never raises: a lost counter costs one id."""
    try:
        value = int(str(contract_id).strip())
    except ValueError:
        hookio.log(f"ledger: cannot count from contract id {contract_id!r}; the counter stands")
        return
    try:
        with open(counter_path(), "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as stream:
            stream.write(str(value))
    except OSError as exc:
        hookio.log(f"ledger: could not write the counter ({exc})")


def _normalise_id(contract_id: Any) -> str | None:
    """A contract number as the four digits everything else prints, or ``None`` when it is not one."""
    if contract_id is None:
        return None
    text = str(contract_id).strip()
    if not text.isdigit():
        return None
    return f"{int(text):0{ID_WIDTH}d}"


def _quarantine(path: Path, reason: str) -> None:
    """Move an unreadable ledger aside so the next contract starts clean, and say where it went."""
    stamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d-%H%M%S")
    target = path.with_name(f"{BAD_PREFIX}{stamp}.json")
    attempt = 1
    while target.exists():
        target = path.with_name(f"{BAD_PREFIX}{stamp}-{attempt}.json")
        attempt += 1
    try:
        path.replace(target)
    except OSError as exc:
        hookio.log(f"ledger: {path.name} is unreadable ({reason}) and could not be moved aside ({exc})")
        return
    hookio.log(f"ledger: {path.name} did not read ({reason}); moved to {target.name}")


def load() -> dict[str, Any] | None:
    """The ledger as it is on disk, or ``None`` when there is none to read.

    A file that is present but not a JSON object is moved to ``ledger.bad.<ts>.json`` and logged:
    the member keeps the bytes, the next contract starts on a clean file.
    """
    path = ledger_path()
    if not path.exists():
        return None
    try:
        with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as stream:
            data = json.load(stream)
    except OSError as exc:
        hookio.log(f"ledger: {path.name} could not be opened ({exc})")
        return None
    except ValueError as exc:
        _quarantine(path, str(exc))
        return None
    if not isinstance(data, dict):
        _quarantine(path, f"the file holds a {type(data).__name__}, not a contract")
        return None
    return data


def save(ledger: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    """Write the ledger whole, crash-atomically."""
    hookio.atomic_write_json(ledger_path(), ledger)


def _rows(ledger: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    rows = ledger.get("rows")
    if not isinstance(rows, list):
        return []
    return [row for row in rows if isinstance(row, dict)]


def _text(value: Any) -> str:
    return str(value).strip() if value is not None else ""


def _is_struck(row: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
    return bool(row.get("struck"))


def _live_rows(ledger: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    """The rows still on the contract: everything the member has not struck."""
    return [row for row in _rows(ledger) if not _is_struck(row)]


def _row_id(value: Any) -> str:
    """A row number as the contract spells it: ``R3`` from ``R3``, ``r3`` or ``3``."""
    text = _text(value)
    if not text:
        return ""
    digits = text.lstrip("Rr")
    return f"R{digits}" if digits.isdigit() else text


def _find_row(ledger: dict[str, Any], row_id: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
    for row in _rows(ledger):
        if _row_id(row.get("id")) == row_id:
            return row
    return None


def _moment(now: datetime | None) -> datetime:
    return now if now is not None else datetime.now()


def _stamp(moment: datetime) -> str:
    return moment.isoformat(timespec="seconds")


def _history_entry(moment: datetime, event: str, text: str, status: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """One line of a row's history: when, what happened, and how the row read at that point."""
    return {"at": _stamp(moment), "event": event, "text": text, "status": status}


def _default_allow_prefixes() -> list[str]:
    """The shipped proof allowlist, read straight from the rules file. Never raises."""
    path = hookio.repo_root().joinpath(*_RULES_RELATIVE)
    data = hookio.read_json(path, {})
    proofs = data.get("proofs") if isinstance(data, dict) else None
    prefixes = proofs.get("allow_prefixes") if isinstance(proofs, dict) else None
    if not isinstance(prefixes, list):
        return []
    return [str(item) for item in prefixes if isinstance(item, str)]


def _names_date(text: str, proof: str) -> bool:
    """True when this row is about a deadline rather than a thing to measure."""
    words = text.strip().lower()
    if _TMINUS_PROOF.match(proof.strip().lower()):
        return True
    if words.startswith("due "):
        return True
    if _TOMORROW in words or _WEEKDAY_WORD.search(words) or _ISO_DATE.search(words):
        return True
    return False


def classify_kind(text: str, proof: str, status: str, allow_prefixes: list[str]) -> str:
    """What kind of row this is, which decides whether the gate can prove it.

    Order matters: a row with no measurable is undefined whatever else it says, a deadline is
    proven by the clock, and only then does the proof string get read as a command. The allowlist
    rule itself is ``proofs.is_allowlisted``, the one the gate re-checks at run time, so a row can
    never be labelled ``auto`` here and refused there.
    """
    proof_text = _text(proof)
    if _text(status) == UNDEFINED_GLYPH or proof_text.lower().startswith(KIND_UNDEFINED):
        return KIND_UNDEFINED
    if _names_date(_text(text), proof_text):
        return KIND_DATE
    if proofs.is_allowlisted(proof_text, allow_prefixes):
        return KIND_AUTO
    return KIND_MANUAL


def _status_for(kind: str, glyph: str) -> str:
    """The status a fresh row starts on: the kind decides, then the glyph the table printed."""
    if kind == KIND_UNDEFINED:
        return UNDEFINED_GLYPH
    if kind == KIND_DATE:
        return ON_TRACK_GLYPH
    return _text(glyph) or OPEN_GLYPH


def due_from_text(text: str, now: datetime) -> datetime | None:
    """The deadline a requirement names, at 17:00 local, or ``None`` when it names none."""
    words = _text(text).lower()
    if not words:
        return None
    iso = _ISO_DATE.search(words)
    if iso:
        try:
            found = datetime(int(iso.group(1)), int(iso.group(2)), int(iso.group(3)))
        except ValueError:
            return None
        return found.replace(hour=DUE_HOUR, minute=0, second=0, microsecond=0)
    day = now.replace(hour=DUE_HOUR, minute=0, second=0, microsecond=0)
    if _TOMORROW in words:
        return day + timedelta(days=1)
    weekday = _WEEKDAY_WORD.search(words)
    if weekday:
        ahead = (WEEKDAYS.index(weekday.group(1)) - now.weekday()) % 7
        return day + timedelta(days=ahead)
    if any(word in words for word in _TODAY_WORDS):
        return day
    return None


def tminus(due: datetime | str | None, now: datetime) -> str:
    """How long is left, floored: ``T-2d 20h``, ``T-5h`` inside the last day, ``T-0h`` once it is up."""
    if not due:
        return ""
    when = due
    if isinstance(when, str):
        try:
            when = datetime.fromisoformat(when)
        except ValueError:
            return ""
    left = when - now
    if left.total_seconds() <= 0:
        return "T-0h"
    hours = left.seconds // 3600
    if left.days <= 0:
        return f"T-{hours}h"
    return f"T-{left.days}d {hours}h"


def _new_row(
    row: tuple[str, str, str, str], allow_prefixes: list[str], moment: datetime
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """One table row as the ledger stores it, with its first line of history."""
    raw_id, text, proof, glyph = row
    row_id = _row_id(raw_id)
    words = _text(text)
    proof_text = _text(proof)
    kind = classify_kind(words, proof_text, _text(glyph), allow_prefixes)
    status = _status_for(kind, _text(glyph))
    return {
        "id": row_id,
        "text": words,
        "proof": proof_text,
        "kind": kind,
        "status": status,
        "struck": False,
        "history": [_history_entry(moment, EVENT_CREATED, words, status)],
        "last_output": "",
        "last_run_at": None,
    }


def _due_for(rows: list[dict[str, Any]], moment: datetime) -> str | None:
    """The first deadline the contract names, as an ISO stamp."""
    for row in rows:
        if row.get("kind") != KIND_DATE:
            continue
        found = due_from_text(_text(row.get("text")), moment) or due_from_text(
            _text(row.get("proof")), moment
        )
        if found is not None:
            return _stamp(found)
    return None


def from_table_rows(
    rows: list[tuple[str, str, str, str]],
    title: str,
    session_id: str,
    allow_prefixes: list[str],
    contract_id: str | None = None,
    now: datetime | None = None,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Write a new contract from the table the model printed, and take its number.

    The id the reply printed wins, because that is the number the member read on screen; the
    counter follows it. With no printed id the counter decides.
    """
    moment = _moment(now)
    number = _normalise_id(contract_id) or next_id()
    built = [_new_row(row, allow_prefixes, moment) for row in rows]
    ledger: dict[str, Any] = {
        "contract_id": number,
        "title": _text(title),
        "created_at": _stamp(moment),
        "approved_at": None,
        "session_id": _text(session_id),
        "due": _due_for(built, moment),
        "rows": built,
        "touched_outside": [],
    }
    save(ledger)
    _write_counter(number)
    return ledger


def update_from_table_rows(
    ledger: dict[str, Any],
    rows: list[tuple[str, str, str, str]],
    now: datetime | None = None,
    allow_prefixes: list[str] | None = None,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Fold a re-printed table into the contract it belongs to.

    A row that reads exactly as before keeps its status and its history, so a re-print never
    un-proves work. A changed row is a new statement of the requirement and resets. A row the
    table no longer carries is struck, not deleted. A row the member already struck stays struck:
    the model re-printing it does not bring it back.
    """
    moment = _moment(now)
    prefixes = allow_prefixes if allow_prefixes is not None else _default_allow_prefixes()
    incoming = {_row_id(row[0]): row for row in rows}
    kept: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
    seen: set[str] = set()

    for row in _rows(ledger):
        row_id = _row_id(row.get("id"))
        row["id"] = row_id
        seen.add(row_id)
        if _is_struck(row):
            kept.append(row)
            continue
        table_row = incoming.get(row_id)
        if table_row is None:
            row["struck"] = True
            history = row.setdefault("history", [])
            if isinstance(history, list):
                history.append(
                    _history_entry(moment, EVENT_STRUCK, _text(row.get("text")), _text(row.get("status")))
                )
            kept.append(row)
            continue
        _, text, proof, glyph = table_row
        words = _text(text)
        proof_text = _text(proof)
        if words == _text(row.get("text")) and proof_text == _text(row.get("proof")):
            kept.append(row)
            continue
        kind = classify_kind(words, proof_text, _text(glyph), prefixes)
        status = _status_for(kind, "")
        row["text"] = words
        row["proof"] = proof_text
        row["kind"] = kind
        row["status"] = status
        history = row.setdefault("history", [])
        if isinstance(history, list):
            history.append(_history_entry(moment, EVENT_REWORDED, words, status))
        kept.append(row)

    for row_id, table_row in incoming.items():
        if row_id in seen:
            continue
        kept.append(_new_row(table_row, prefixes, moment))

    ledger["rows"] = kept
    if not ledger.get("due"):
        ledger["due"] = _due_for(kept, moment)
    save(ledger)
    return ledger


class _Edit(NamedTuple):
    """One numbered edit: which row, its new wording and its new proof when the member gave them."""

    row_id: str
    text: str | None = None
    proof: str | None = None
    strike: bool = False


def _split_proof(tail: str) -> tuple[str | None, str | None]:
    """A reword tail as ``(wording, proof)``. Either half may be missing: the member decides."""
    proof_first = _PROOF_HEAD.match(tail)
    if proof_first:
        return None, proof_first.group(1).strip() or None
    found = _PROOF_TAIL.search(tail)
    if found is None:
        return tail.strip() or None, None
    return tail[: found.start()].strip() or None, found.group(1).strip() or None


def _match_edit(prompt: str) -> _Edit | None:
    """The edit the member typed, or ``None`` when the prompt is an ordinary turn."""
    text = prompt or ""
    strike = _STRIKE.match(text)
    if strike:
        return _Edit(row_id=f"R{int(strike.group(1))}", strike=True)
    measure = _PROOF_ONLY.match(text)
    if measure:
        return _Edit(row_id=f"R{int(measure.group(1))}", proof=measure.group(2).strip() or None)
    for pattern in (_REWORD, _COLON):
        found = pattern.match(text)
        if found is None:
            continue
        words, proof = _split_proof(found.group(2))
        if words is None and proof is None:
            return None
        return _Edit(row_id=f"R{int(found.group(1))}", text=words, proof=proof)
    return None


def is_edit(prompt: str) -> bool:
    """True when this prompt edits the contract by number rather than asking for something."""
    return _match_edit(prompt) is not None


def apply_edit(
    prompt: str, now: datetime | None = None, allow_prefixes: list[str] | None = None
) -> str | None:
    """Apply a numbered edit and say what happened, or ``None`` when there is nothing to apply.

    A member can change a row's wording, its proof, or both in one line. A new proof is a new
    measure, so the row is classified again and owed again: the old status belonged to the old
    command. Either edit also lifts a strike, because the member has just re-stated the row.
    Nothing is deleted, the history keeps every move under its own name.
    """
    edit = _match_edit(prompt)
    if edit is None:
        return None
    ledger = load()
    if ledger is None:
        return None
    row = _find_row(ledger, edit.row_id)
    if row is None:
        return f"{edit.row_id} is not on the contract"

    moment = _moment(now)
    history = row.setdefault("history", [])
    if edit.strike:
        row["struck"] = True
        if isinstance(history, list):
            history.append(
                _history_entry(moment, EVENT_STRUCK, _text(row.get("text")), _text(row.get("status")))
            )
        save(ledger)
        return f"{edit.row_id} struck"

    events: list[str] = []
    if edit.text is not None:
        row["text"] = edit.text
        events.append(EVENT_REWORDED)
    if edit.proof is not None:
        prefixes = allow_prefixes if allow_prefixes is not None else _default_allow_prefixes()
        row["proof"] = edit.proof
        row["kind"] = classify_kind(_text(row.get("text")), edit.proof, "", prefixes)
        events.append(EVENT_REPROOFED)

    status = _status_for(_text(row.get("kind")), "")
    row["status"] = status
    row["struck"] = False
    if isinstance(history, list):
        for event in events:
            history.append(_history_entry(moment, event, _text(row.get("text")), status))
    save(ledger)
    return f"{edit.row_id} {' and '.join(events)}"


def approve(session_id: str, now: datetime | None = None) -> bool:
    """Mark the contract agreed. True the first time, False every time after: go is said once."""
    ledger = load()
    if ledger is None or ledger.get("approved_at"):
        return False
    moment = _moment(now)
    ledger["approved_at"] = _stamp(moment)
    if _text(session_id):
        ledger["session_id"] = _text(session_id)
    for row in _live_rows(ledger):
        history = row.setdefault("history", [])
        if isinstance(history, list):
            history.append(
                _history_entry(moment, EVENT_APPROVED, _text(row.get("text")), _text(row.get("status")))
            )
    save(ledger)
    return True


def stamp(row_id: str, status: str, output: str, now: datetime | None = None) -> None:
    """Record what a proof did to one row: its status, its output and when it ran."""
    ledger = load()
    if ledger is None:
        hookio.log(f"ledger: nothing to stamp {row_id} on; there is no contract")
        return
    wanted = _row_id(row_id)
    row = _find_row(ledger, wanted)
    if row is None:
        hookio.log(f"ledger: {wanted} is not on the contract, so its proof was not stamped")
        return
    moment = _moment(now)
    row["status"] = _text(status)
    row["last_output"] = str(output or "")[:MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS]
    row["last_run_at"] = _stamp(moment)
    history = row.setdefault("history", [])
    if isinstance(history, list):
        history.append(_history_entry(moment, EVENT_PROOF, _text(row.get("text")), _text(status)))
    save(ledger)


def rows_open(ledger: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    """The rows still owed: on the contract, and not yet proven."""
    return [row for row in _live_rows(ledger) if _text(row.get("status")) != PROVEN_GLYPH]


def _view_line(row: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """One ledger row as the injection shows it."""
    row_id = _row_id(row.get("id"))
    words = _text(row.get("text"))
    if _is_struck(row):
        return f"{ROW_INDENT}{row_id}{STRUCK_SEPARATOR}{words}"
    head = " ".join(part for part in (row_id, _text(row.get("status")), words) if part)
    proof = _text(row.get("proof"))
    return ROW_INDENT + head + (PROOF_SEPARATOR + proof if proof else "")


def view(max_rows: int = DEFAULT_MAX_ROWS) -> str:
    """The ledger as the injection shows it: one line per row, empty text when there is no ledger."""
    ledger = load()
    if ledger is None:
        return ""
    rows = _rows(ledger)
    shown = rows[: max_rows if max_rows > 0 else 0]
    lines = [_view_line(row) for row in shown]
    held_back = len(rows) - len(shown)
    if held_back > 0:
        lines.append(MORE_LINE.format(count=held_back))
    return "\n".join(lines)


def _status_counts(rows: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> dict[str, int]:
    """How many live rows sit on each status."""
    counts: dict[str, int] = {}
    for row in rows:
        status = _text(row.get("status"))
        counts[status] = counts.get(status, 0) + 1
    return counts


def lite_lines(now: datetime | None = None) -> list[str]:
    """The two data lines the SITREP-lite frame reads out beside the cat.

    ``now`` is accepted so a caller can pin the moment; these two lines carry no clock of their own.
    """
    del now  # why: the frame's third line is the model's, and it is the only one that reads the clock
    ledger = load()
    if ledger is None:
        return []
    rows = _live_rows(ledger)
    counts = _status_counts(rows)
    head = [CONTRACT_LINE.format(contract_id=_text(ledger.get("contract_id")) or next_id())]
    title = _text(ledger.get("title"))
    if title:
        head.append(title)
    # why: the manual mark IS its word, so it is never printed twice
    parts = [
        f"{counts[glyph]} {word}" if glyph == word else f"{counts[glyph]} {glyph} {word}"
        for glyph, word in COUNT_WORDS
        if counts.get(glyph)
    ]
    lines = [LINE_JOIN.join(head)]
    if parts:
        lines.append(LINE_JOIN.join(parts))
    return lines


def _catches(ledger: dict[str, Any]) -> int:
    """How many rows the gate ever caught failing. A strike does not undo a catch that happened."""
    total = 0
    for row in _rows(ledger):
        history = row.get("history")
        if not isinstance(history, list):
            continue
        if any(
            isinstance(entry, dict) and _text(entry.get("status")) == FAILED_GLYPH for entry in history
        ):
            total += 1
    return total


def summary() -> dict[str, Any] | None:
    """The counts the skins put in their bars, or ``None`` when there is no ledger."""
    ledger = load()
    if ledger is None:
        return None
    rows = _live_rows(ledger)
    statuses = [_text(row.get("status")) for row in rows]
    total = len(rows)
    built = sum(1 for status in statuses if status in BUILT_GLYPHS)
    proven = sum(1 for status in statuses if status == PROVEN_GLYPH)
    due = ledger.get("due")
    return {
        "contract_id": _text(ledger.get("contract_id")) or next_id(),
        "title": _text(ledger.get("title")),
        "total": total,
        "built": built,
        "proven": proven,
        "open": total - built,
        "approved": bool(ledger.get("approved_at")),
        "due": due if isinstance(due, str) and due else None,
        "catches": _catches(ledger),
    }



================================================
FILE: .claude/hooks/lib/proofs.py
================================================
"""Run a contract's proofs, and notice what got touched outside the contract.

Two facts must both be true before anything executes: the member approved the contract (the gate
owns that), and the command's opening tokens are on the allowlist in ``rules.json`` (this module
owns that). The allowlist is re-checked at run time, not at write time, so an edit to the rules
between the approval and the run is honoured. Anything else is stamped ``manual`` and never
reaches a shell.

The command string is always passed as ONE argument to the chosen shell (``bash -lc``,
``powershell -NoProfile -Command`` or ``cmd /c``); ``shell=True`` never appears here, so a proof
carrying quotes or spaces runs as written and nothing re-parses it on the way. Output is
collected in a temp file rather than a pipe: killing a timed-out proof leaves grandchildren
holding the pipe ends open, and closing a pipe under them blocks for as long as the runaway
command lives, which would hand the member a hung session instead of a ``⏱``.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import shutil
import subprocess
import tempfile
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import PurePosixPath
from typing import Any

from lib import hookio

PASS_GLYPH = "●"  # why: the ledger's mark for a proof that ran and returned zero
FAIL_GLYPH = "✗"  # why: the ledger's mark for a proof that ran and returned non-zero
TIMEOUT_GLYPH = "⏱"  # why: a proof that ran out of time is not a failure, so it gets its own mark
MANUAL_STATUS = "manual"  # why: a human check or a command we will not execute; a word, not a glyph
AUTO_KIND = "auto"  # why: the only row kind this module ever executes

OUTPUT_LIMIT = 400  # why: the gate quotes the first 120 characters, so 400 keeps context without bloat
READ_LIMIT = OUTPUT_LIMIT * 4  # why: four bytes is the widest UTF-8 character, so this always covers the limit
DEFAULT_PER_PROOF_SECONDS = 15.0  # why: the per-proof cap in rules.json, for rules that carry none
DEFAULT_TOTAL_SECONDS = 45.0  # why: the whole-run cap in rules.json; the Stop hook's own budget is 60 s
GIT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 10.0  # why: a status read on a healthy repo is instant; ten seconds means trouble

# why: a prefix matches only when the command stops spelling the same word: "pythonx" is not
# "python" and "curl http://localhost.evil.com" is not "curl http://localhost", while a port or a
# path straight after the prefix ("curl http://localhost:3000/health") is the same target and stays
# allowed. Alphanumerics are caught by str.isalnum(), these are the rest of a word's characters.
WORD_CHARS = "_-."

# why: named so the gate's telemetry line and the Result rows agree on what "no shell" is called
NO_SHELL = "none"
NO_SHELL_OUTPUT = "no shell found on PATH"

PORCELAIN_PATH_START = 3  # why: `git status --porcelain` prints two status characters and a space
RENAME_ARROW = " -> "  # why: a renamed path is printed as "old -> new"; the new name is the one on disk


@dataclass
class Result:
    """One row's proof after the run: what happened, what it said, how long it took."""

    row_id: str
    status: str
    output: str
    seconds: float
    command: str
    shell: str


EXPECT_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^(?P<command>.*?)\s*(?:→|->)\s*(?P<lines>\d+)\s*$")
# why: the signed contract writes a proof as `<command> → 0`; the arrow says how many lines the
# command must print, and grep-style tools exit 1 on no match, so the line count is the verdict


def split_expectation(proof: str) -> tuple[str, int | None]:
    """The runnable command and the expected line count, or ``None`` when the proof has no arrow."""
    text = (proof or "").strip()
    match = EXPECT_PATTERN.match(text)
    if match is None:
        return (text, None)
    return (match.group("command").strip(), int(match.group("lines")))


def is_allowlisted(command: str, allow_prefixes: list[str]) -> bool:
    """True when the command (arrow stripped) opens with an allowlisted prefix on a word boundary."""
    text = split_expectation(command)[0].lower()
    if not text:
        return False
    for prefix in allow_prefixes or []:
        if not isinstance(prefix, str):
            continue
        head = prefix.strip().lower()
        if not head or not text.startswith(head):
            continue
        tail = text[len(head) :]
        if not tail or not (tail[0].isalnum() or tail[0] in WORD_CHARS):
            return True
    return False


def choose_shell() -> tuple[str, list[str]]:
    """The shell the proofs run through: the member's own, never one we bundle.

    ``bash`` first (Git Bash on Windows, native on Mac and Linux), then PowerShell, then ``cmd``.
    The name comes back with the argv so the gate can say in telemetry which one ran.
    """
    bash = shutil.which("bash")
    if bash:
        return ("bash", [bash, "-lc"])
    powershell = shutil.which("powershell")
    if powershell:
        return ("powershell", [powershell, "-NoProfile", "-Command"])
    command_shell = shutil.which("cmd")
    if command_shell:
        return ("cmd", [command_shell, "/c"])
    return (NO_SHELL, [])


def run_one(
    command: str,
    cwd: str,
    timeout_s: float,
    shell: tuple[str, list[str]] | None = None,
) -> tuple[str, str, float]:
    """Run one command in ``cwd`` and report ``(status, output, seconds)``. Never raises.

    A proof written ``<command> → N`` passes when the command prints exactly N non-empty lines,
    whatever its exit code; a proof with no arrow passes on exit 0.
    """
    _name, argv = shell if shell is not None else choose_shell()
    if not argv:
        return (FAIL_GLYPH, NO_SHELL_OUTPUT, 0.0)
    command, expected_lines = split_expectation(command)

    started = time.monotonic()
    try:
        with tempfile.TemporaryFile(mode="w+b") as sink:
            process = subprocess.Popen(
                [*argv, command],
                cwd=str(cwd),
                stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL,  # why: a proof that waits for input would hang the gate
                stdout=sink,
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,  # why: one stream keeps both outputs in the order they happened
                shell=False,
            )
            try:
                process.wait(timeout=timeout_s)
            except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
                process.kill()
                process.wait()
                return (TIMEOUT_GLYPH, f"timed out after {_seconds_word(timeout_s)}", float(timeout_s))
            output = _sink_text(sink)
            if expected_lines is None:
                status = PASS_GLYPH if process.returncode == 0 else FAIL_GLYPH
                return (status, output, time.monotonic() - started)
            printed = len([line for line in output.splitlines() if line.strip()])
            # why: exit 0 and 1 are "found" and "not found" for grep-style tools; anything above 1
            # is the tool itself failing (a missing path, a bad flag), and its error line must
            # never be counted as a match
            broke = process.returncode not in (0, 1)
            status = PASS_GLYPH if printed == expected_lines and not broke else FAIL_GLYPH
            counted = f"{printed} lines, wanted {expected_lines}"
            return (status, (counted + (": " + output if output else ""))[:OUTPUT_LIMIT], time.monotonic() - started)
    except OSError as exc:
        hookio.log(f"proofs: {command!r} did not start ({exc})")
        return (FAIL_GLYPH, f"could not run it: {exc}"[:OUTPUT_LIMIT], time.monotonic() - started)


def run_all(ledger: dict[str, Any], cwd: str, rules: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Result]:
    """Run every runnable proof on the contract, in order, inside the whole-run budget.

    A row runs only when it is not struck, its kind is ``auto`` and its command is still
    allowlisted. Everything else comes back ``manual`` with nothing executed. Once the budget is
    spent the remaining runnable rows come back ``⏱`` without running: a Stop hook that overruns
    its own timeout is worse than an unproven row.
    """
    settings = rules.get("proofs") if isinstance(rules, dict) else None
    settings = settings if isinstance(settings, dict) else {}
    allow_prefixes = settings.get("allow_prefixes")
    allow_prefixes = allow_prefixes if isinstance(allow_prefixes, list) else []
    per_proof = _positive_float(settings.get("per_proof_seconds"), DEFAULT_PER_PROOF_SECONDS)
    budget = _positive_float(settings.get("total_seconds"), DEFAULT_TOTAL_SECONDS)

    shell = choose_shell()
    shell_name = shell[0]
    results: list[Result] = []
    spent = 0.0

    for row in _rows(ledger):
        if row.get("struck"):
            continue
        row_id = _text(row.get("id"))
        command = _text(row.get("proof"))
        if row.get("kind") != AUTO_KIND or not is_allowlisted(command, allow_prefixes):
            results.append(Result(row_id, MANUAL_STATUS, "", 0.0, command, shell_name))
            continue
        if spent >= budget:
            message = f"not run: the proof run passed its {_seconds_word(budget)} budget"
            results.append(Result(row_id, TIMEOUT_GLYPH, message, 0.0, command, shell_name))
            continue
        status, output, seconds = run_one(command, cwd, per_proof, shell)
        spent += seconds
        results.append(Result(row_id, status, output, round(seconds, 3), command, shell_name))

    return results


def git_touched_outside(cwd: str, ledger: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:
    """Changed files no row on the contract names, in the order git reports them.

    Advisory only: it answers "what else moved while this was built". A folder that is not a git
    repo, or a machine with no git, gives an empty list rather than an error.
    """
    git = shutil.which("git")
    if not git:
        return []
    try:
        done = subprocess.run(
            [git, "status", "--porcelain"],
            cwd=str(cwd),
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
            encoding="utf-8",
            errors="replace",
            timeout=GIT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
            check=False,
        )
    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError) as exc:
        hookio.log(f"proofs: git status did not run in {cwd} ({exc})")
        return []
    if done.returncode != 0:
        return []

    named = _named_text(ledger)
    outside: list[str] = []
    for line in (done.stdout or "").splitlines():
        path = _porcelain_path(line)
        if not path or path in outside or _is_named(path, named):
            continue
        outside.append(path)
    return outside


def _sink_text(sink: Any) -> str:
    """The head of a finished command's output, decoded and clipped."""
    try:
        sink.seek(0)
        raw = sink.read(READ_LIMIT)
    except OSError:
        return ""
    return raw.decode("utf-8", errors="replace")[:OUTPUT_LIMIT]


def _seconds_word(value: float) -> str:
    """``1.5`` as ``1.5s`` and ``15.0`` as ``15s``: the wording the gate's reason carries."""
    return f"{float(value):g}s"


def _positive_float(value: Any, fallback: float) -> float:
    """A number from the rules, or the fallback when it is missing, unreadable or not positive."""
    try:
        number = float(value)
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return fallback
    return number if number > 0 else fallback


def _rows(ledger: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    """The ledger's rows, skipping anything that is not a row."""
    rows = ledger.get("rows") if isinstance(ledger, dict) else None
    if not isinstance(rows, list):
        return []
    return [row for row in rows if isinstance(row, dict)]


def _text(value: Any) -> str:
    return str(value).strip() if value is not None else ""


def _named_text(ledger: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """Every non-struck row's requirement and proof, lowercased, as one blob to search."""
    parts: list[str] = []
    for row in _rows(ledger):
        if row.get("struck"):
            continue
        parts.append(_text(row.get("text")))
        parts.append(_text(row.get("proof")))
    return " ".join(parts).lower()


def _is_named(path: str, named: str) -> bool:
    """True when the contract mentions this path, either in full or by its file name."""
    if not named:
        return False
    lowered = path.lower()
    if lowered in named:
        return True
    base = PurePosixPath(lowered.replace("\\", "/")).name
    return bool(base) and base in named


def _porcelain_path(line: str) -> str:
    """The path out of one ``git status --porcelain`` line, renames resolved to the new name."""
    if len(line) <= PORCELAIN_PATH_START:
        return ""
    path = line[PORCELAIN_PATH_START:].strip()
    if RENAME_ARROW in path:
        path = path.split(RENAME_ARROW, 1)[1].strip()
    if len(path) >= 2 and path.startswith('"') and path.endswith('"'):
        path = path[1:-1]
    return path



================================================
FILE: .claude/hooks/lib/rules.py
================================================
"""Read algorithm/rules.json and refuse it plainly when a value cannot be used.

The router calls load_rules() once per turn. Anything wrong in the file raises
RulesError with the offending key at the front of the message, so the member
reads one line and knows which number to change. router.py catches RulesError
and carries on with FALLBACK_RULES.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import re
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, NoReturn

from lib.hookio import repo_root

CLASSIFIERS = ("rules", "haiku")  # why: the only two deciders the repo ships, one local and one model call
RULES_DIRNAME = "algorithm"  # why: the algorithm sits in its own folder so a member can read it without opening .claude
RULES_FILENAME = "rules.json"  # why: one file holds the whole algorithm, named in the README
SKINS_DIRNAME = "skins"  # why: one markdown file per skin, keyed by the skin name
SKIN_SUFFIX = ".md"  # why: skins are markdown so they read as text on GitHub
CLAUSE_KEYS = ("per_clause", "min", "cap")  # why: a per-clause weight needs a step, a floor and a ceiling
WEIGHT_MIN = 0.0  # why: a threshold or tie band outside 0..1 can never fire, so it is a typo not a tuning
WEIGHT_MAX = 1.0  # why: signals return 0.0 to 1.0, so weights share that scale


class RulesError(ValueError):
    """rules.json is missing a key, or carries a value the router cannot use."""


def rules_path() -> Path:
    """Where the shipped algorithm lives."""
    return repo_root() / RULES_DIRNAME / RULES_FILENAME


def _skins_dir() -> Path:
    """Where the skin files live. A module-level function so tests can point it elsewhere."""
    return repo_root() / SKINS_DIRNAME


def _fail(key: str, what: str) -> NoReturn:
    raise RulesError(f"{key}: {what}")


def _is_number(value: Any) -> bool:
    return isinstance(value, (int, float)) and not isinstance(value, bool)


def _check_weight(label: str, weight: Any) -> None:
    if _is_number(weight):
        return
    if isinstance(weight, dict):
        missing = [key for key in CLAUSE_KEYS if key not in weight]
        if missing:
            _fail(label, "is missing " + ", ".join(missing))
        wrong = [key for key in CLAUSE_KEYS if not _is_number(weight[key])]
        if wrong:
            _fail(label, ", ".join(wrong) + " must be numbers")
        return
    _fail(label, "must be a number, or an object with per_clause, min and cap")


def _check_skins(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    skins = rules.get("skins")
    if not isinstance(skins, dict) or not skins:
        _fail("skins", "must be an object holding at least one skin")

    default_skin = rules.get("default_skin")
    if not isinstance(default_skin, str) or default_skin not in skins:
        _fail("default_skin", "must name one of the skins: " + ", ".join(skins))

    skins_dir = _skins_dir()
    for name, spec in skins.items():
        label = f"skins.{name}"
        if not isinstance(spec, dict):
            _fail(label, "must be an object with tier and signals")
        tier = spec.get("tier")
        if not isinstance(tier, str) or not tier.strip():
            _fail(f"{label}.tier", "must be a short label such as E3")
        signals = spec.get("signals")
        if not isinstance(signals, dict) or not signals:
            _fail(f"{label}.signals", "must be an object of signal names and weights")
        for signal, weight in signals.items():
            _check_weight(f"{label}.signals.{signal}", weight)
        skin_file = skins_dir / f"{name}{SKIN_SUFFIX}"
        if not skin_file.is_file():
            _fail(label, f"no skin file at {skin_file}")
    return skins


def _check_explicit(rules: dict[str, Any], skins: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    explicit = rules.get("explicit")
    if not isinstance(explicit, dict):
        _fail("explicit", "must be an object with trailing_tokens and whole_prompt")
    for group in ("trailing_tokens", "whole_prompt"):
        mapping = explicit.get(group)
        if not isinstance(mapping, dict):
            _fail(f"explicit.{group}", "must be an object mapping a token to a skin")
        for token, skin in mapping.items():
            if not isinstance(skin, str) or skin not in skins:
                _fail(f"explicit.{group}.{token}", f"points at {skin!r}, which is not one of the skins")


def _check_house(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    house = rules.get("house")
    if house is None:
        return
    if not isinstance(house, dict):
        _fail("house", "must be an object with set_pattern")
    pattern = house.get("set_pattern")
    if not isinstance(pattern, str) or not pattern:
        _fail("house.set_pattern", "must be a regular expression written as text")
    try:
        re.compile(pattern)
    except re.error as exc:
        _fail("house.set_pattern", f"is not a valid regular expression ({exc.msg})")


def _check_proofs(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    proofs = rules.get("proofs")
    if not isinstance(proofs, dict):
        _fail("proofs", "must be an object with allow_prefixes")
    allow = proofs.get("allow_prefixes")
    if not isinstance(allow, list) or any(not isinstance(prefix, str) or not prefix.strip() for prefix in allow):
        _fail("proofs.allow_prefixes", "must be a list of command prefixes written as text")
    for key in ("per_proof_seconds", "total_seconds"):
        if key in proofs and (not _is_number(proofs[key]) or proofs[key] <= 0):
            _fail(f"proofs.{key}", "must be a number of seconds above zero")


def _check_lexicons(rules: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    lexicons = rules.get("lexicons")
    if lexicons is None:
        return
    if not isinstance(lexicons, dict):
        _fail("lexicons", "must be an object of word lists")
    for name, words in lexicons.items():
        if not isinstance(words, list) or any(not isinstance(word, str) for word in words):
            _fail(f"lexicons.{name}", "must be a list of words written as text")


def validate_rules(rules: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Return the rules unchanged, or raise RulesError naming the key that is wrong."""
    if not isinstance(rules, dict):
        _fail(RULES_FILENAME, "the top level must be an object")

    version = rules.get("version")
    if not isinstance(version, int) or isinstance(version, bool):
        _fail("version", "must be a whole number")

    classifier = rules.get("classifier")
    if classifier not in CLASSIFIERS:
        _fail("classifier", 'must be "rules" or "haiku"')

    for key in ("threshold", "tie_band"):
        value = rules.get(key)
        if not _is_number(value) or not WEIGHT_MIN <= float(value) <= WEIGHT_MAX:
            _fail(key, f"must be a number between {WEIGHT_MIN} and {WEIGHT_MAX}")

    skins = _check_skins(rules)
    _check_explicit(rules, skins)
    _check_house(rules)
    _check_proofs(rules)
    _check_lexicons(rules)
    return rules


def load_rules(path: Path | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Read rules.json and validate it. Raises RulesError on anything unusable."""
    target = Path(path) if path is not None else rules_path()
    try:
        raw = target.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    except OSError as exc:
        _fail(RULES_FILENAME, f"cannot read {target} ({exc.strerror or exc})")
    try:
        parsed = json.loads(raw)
    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
        _fail(RULES_FILENAME, f"is not valid JSON, line {exc.lineno} column {exc.colno}")
    return validate_rules(parsed)


# why: when rules.json is unreadable the router still has to answer, so this is the smallest
# algorithm that keeps a session working: one skin, local deciding, no proofs run unattended.
FALLBACK_RULES: dict[str, Any] = {
    "version": 1,
    "classifier": "rules",
    "threshold": 0.35,
    "tie_band": 0.10,
    "default_skin": "bluf",
    "skins": {"bluf": {"tier": "E2", "signals": {"question_shape": 0.25}}},
    "explicit": {"trailing_tokens": {"/bluf": "bluf"}, "whole_prompt": {}},
    "proofs": {"allow_prefixes": [], "per_proof_seconds": 15, "total_seconds": 45},
}



================================================
FILE: .claude/hooks/lib/score.py
================================================
"""The routing decision: what the member typed, and what answers when nothing does.

Two certain edges and a scored middle. An explicit token (`/sitrep`, `/e5`, or the bare word `go`)
is not a guess, so it scores 1.0 and beats everything. When nothing speaks at all, the house floor
or the rules' own default skin answers at 0.0, which is the honest reading rather than a low guess.
Between them sits ``decide()``: every skin's signals are weighted and summed, the skins below the
threshold drop out, the top one wins, and a runner-up inside the tie band is carried on the
decision so the model can pick from the conversation. Every path returns the same ``Decision``.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any

if TYPE_CHECKING:  # why: the signals module is imported only where it is used, so this stays a name
    from lib.signals import State

EXPLICIT_SCORE = 1.0  # why: the member typed the token, so there is nothing left to be unsure about
DEFAULT_SCORE = 0.0  # why: no signal fired, and a made-up number here would read as confidence
UNKNOWN_TIER = "E?"  # why: a skin the rules do not list has no tier, and a guessed one would read as fact
FALLBACK_SKIN = "bluf"  # why: the same skin rules.FALLBACK_RULES ships, so a broken file still names one that exists
SOURCE_EXPLICIT = "explicit"  # why: the sources are written on the router line, so they are spelled once here
SOURCE_HOUSE = "house"  # why: the member's standing floor answered, not the rules' default
SOURCE_DEFAULT = "default"  # why: the rules' own default_skin answered
SOURCE_RULES = "rules"  # why: the scored middle answered, so the router line can say where it came from
TOP_SIGNALS = 3  # why: the injection shows three signals on one line, which is what fits and what reads
SCORE_DP = 2  # why: the router line prints two decimals, so the stored number is the one the member reads
THRESHOLD_FALLBACK = 0.35  # why: the shipped rules' own threshold, for a rules dict that arrives without one
TIE_BAND_FALLBACK = 0.10  # why: the shipped rules' own tie band, same reason

# why: /e1../e5 name the tier in the token itself, so the token's number wins over the skin's own tier
_TIER_TOKEN = re.compile(r"^/e(\d+)$")


@dataclass
class Decision:
    """One routing decision: which skin, at what tier, how sure, why, and what nearly won."""

    skin: str
    tier: str
    score: float
    source: str
    signals_top3: list[tuple[str, float]] = field(default_factory=list)
    tie_with: str | None = None


def tier_of(skin: str, rules: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """The tier label the rules give this skin, or ``E?`` when they do not list it."""
    skins = rules.get("skins")
    spec = skins.get(skin) if isinstance(skins, dict) else None
    tier = spec.get("tier") if isinstance(spec, dict) else None
    if isinstance(tier, str) and tier.strip():
        return tier
    return UNKNOWN_TIER


def explicit_decision(prompt: str, rules: dict[str, Any]) -> Decision | None:
    """The skin the member named outright, or ``None`` when they named none.

    Two spellings, checked in this order:

    - a trailing token: the last whitespace-separated word, lowercased, is one of
      ``explicit.trailing_tokens``. A prompt that is only that token counts too, which is how
      ``/e5`` on its own opens a contract with an empty rant.
    - a whole prompt: the whole prompt, stripped and lowercased, is one of
      ``explicit.whole_prompt``. These are bare words (``sitrep``, ``go``) because Claude Code
      would swallow a leading-slash prompt as its own slash command.

    A trailing ``/e<n>`` carries its own tier, so ``what time is it /e5`` is the contract skin at
    E5 even though the contract skin's own tier is E4.
    """
    explicit = rules.get("explicit")
    if not isinstance(explicit, dict):
        return None
    trailing = explicit.get("trailing_tokens")
    whole = explicit.get("whole_prompt")
    trailing = trailing if isinstance(trailing, dict) else {}
    whole = whole if isinstance(whole, dict) else {}

    text = prompt.strip()
    if not text:
        return None
    tokens = text.split()

    last = tokens[-1].lower()
    skin = trailing.get(last)
    if isinstance(skin, str) and skin:
        token_tier = _TIER_TOKEN.match(last)
        tier = f"E{token_tier.group(1)}" if token_tier else tier_of(skin, rules)
        return Decision(skin=skin, tier=tier, score=EXPLICIT_SCORE, source=SOURCE_EXPLICIT)

    skin = whole.get(text.lower())
    if isinstance(skin, str) and skin:
        return Decision(
            skin=skin, tier=tier_of(skin, rules), score=EXPLICIT_SCORE, source=SOURCE_EXPLICIT
        )
    return None


def default_decision(rules: dict[str, Any], house_skin: str | None = None) -> Decision:
    """The skin that answers when nothing else did: the house floor if one is set, else the default."""
    if house_skin:
        return Decision(
            skin=house_skin,
            tier=tier_of(house_skin, rules),
            score=DEFAULT_SCORE,
            source=SOURCE_HOUSE,
        )
    default_skin = rules.get("default_skin")
    skin = default_skin if isinstance(default_skin, str) and default_skin else FALLBACK_SKIN
    return Decision(
        skin=skin, tier=tier_of(skin, rules), score=DEFAULT_SCORE, source=SOURCE_DEFAULT
    )


def _number(value: Any, fallback: float) -> float:
    """A number from the rules file, or the fallback when the file gave something unusable."""
    if isinstance(value, (int, float)) and not isinstance(value, bool):
        return float(value)
    return fallback


def _contribution(weight: Any, value: float) -> float:
    """What one signal adds to one skin's total.

    A plain weight multiplies the signal. A per-clause weight (``{per_clause, min, cap}``) reads a
    raw count instead: below ``min`` it adds nothing, above it the count is paid per clause up to
    ``cap``. That is how a three-item rant opens a contract while a two-clause sentence does not.
    """
    if isinstance(weight, dict):
        if value < _number(weight.get("min"), 0.0):
            return 0.0
        return min(_number(weight.get("cap"), 0.0), _number(weight.get("per_clause"), 0.0) * value)
    return _number(weight, 0.0) * value


def _computed_signals(prompt: str, state: State, rules: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Compute every signal the rules name. Imported here so score.py stands on its own."""
    from lib import signals as signals_module

    return signals_module.all_signals(prompt, state, rules)


def score_skins(
    prompt: str,
    state: State,
    rules: dict[str, Any],
    signals: dict[str, float] | None = None,
) -> dict[str, tuple[float, list[tuple[str, float]]]]:
    """Score every skin: ``{skin: (total, [(signal, contribution) ...])}``, contributions desc.

    Pass ``signals`` to score a set of values you already have; leave it out and the signals are
    read from the prompt and the state. A signal a skin names but nothing produced counts as 0.0,
    and a contribution of 0.0 is left off the list: the list says what moved the score. Equal
    contributions keep the order the rules file wrote them in, so the same prompt always renders
    the same line.
    """
    values = signals if signals is not None else _computed_signals(prompt, state, rules)
    skins = rules.get("skins")
    if not isinstance(skins, dict):
        return {}

    scored: dict[str, tuple[float, list[tuple[str, float]]]] = {}
    for name, spec in skins.items():
        weights = spec.get("signals") if isinstance(spec, dict) else None
        if not isinstance(weights, dict):
            scored[name] = (0.0, [])
            continue
        total = 0.0
        contributions: list[tuple[str, float]] = []
        for signal_name, weight in weights.items():
            amount = _contribution(weight, _number(values.get(signal_name), 0.0))
            total += amount
            if amount:
                contributions.append((signal_name, amount))
        contributions.sort(key=lambda item: -item[1])
        scored[name] = (total, contributions)
    return scored


def decide(
    prompt: str,
    state: State,
    rules: dict[str, Any],
    house_skin: str | None = None,
    signals: dict[str, float] | None = None,
) -> Decision:
    """The whole algorithm in one call: explicit, then the scored middle, then the floor.

    An explicit token returns straight away at 1.0. Otherwise every skin is scored and the ones
    below ``threshold`` drop out. Nothing left means nothing spoke, so the house floor or the
    default skin answers at 0.0, carrying the best skin's signals anyway so the injection can show
    why nothing cleared. Otherwise the top skin wins, and the second one is named as ``tie_with``
    when it sits inside ``tie_band``, which hands that one call to the model.
    """
    explicit = explicit_decision(prompt, rules)
    if explicit is not None:
        return explicit

    scored = score_skins(prompt, state, rules, signals)
    # why: ties on the total resolve by name, so one prompt can never route two ways
    ranked = sorted(scored.items(), key=lambda item: (-item[1][0], item[0]))
    threshold = _number(rules.get("threshold"), THRESHOLD_FALLBACK)
    candidates = [row for row in ranked if row[1][0] >= threshold]

    if not candidates:
        decision = default_decision(rules, house_skin)
        if ranked:
            decision.signals_top3 = ranked[0][1][1][:TOP_SIGNALS]
        return decision

    skin, (total, contributions) = candidates[0]
    tie_with: str | None = None
    if len(candidates) > 1:
        runner_up, (runner_up_total, _) = candidates[1]
        if total - runner_up_total <= _number(rules.get("tie_band"), TIE_BAND_FALLBACK):
            tie_with = runner_up
    return Decision(
        skin=skin,
        tier=tier_of(skin, rules),
        score=round(total, SCORE_DP),
        source=SOURCE_RULES,
        signals_top3=contributions[:TOP_SIGNALS],
        tie_with=tie_with,
    )


def margin(
    prompt: str,
    state: State,
    rules: dict[str, Any],
    signals: dict[str, float] | None = None,
) -> float:
    """How far the winning skin sits above the next one, at the two decimals the router prints.

    An explicit token is a certainty, so its margin is 1.0. A winner with no second skin scores
    against 0.0. This is the number the demo prompts have to clear.
    """
    if explicit_decision(prompt, rules) is not None:
        return EXPLICIT_SCORE
    scored = score_skins(prompt, state, rules, signals)
    totals = sorted((total for total, _ in scored.values()), reverse=True)
    if not totals:
        return DEFAULT_SCORE
    runner_up_total = totals[1] if len(totals) > 1 else 0.0
    return round(totals[0] - runner_up_total, SCORE_DP)



================================================
FILE: .claude/hooks/lib/signals.py
================================================
"""What a prompt looks like, read as numbers.

Each signal answers one small question about the text the member typed, and answers it the same way
every time: 1.0 for yes, 0.0 for no, and the fraction in between where a signal counts hits. Nothing
here decides anything. ``score.py`` multiplies these numbers by the weights in ``algorithm/rules.json``
and picks the skin, so tuning the router means changing a weight or a word list, never this file.

Two things are deliberate:

- **The words live in the rules, not here.** Every lexicon comes from ``rules["lexicons"]``. The word
  lists in this file are the ones the shipped rules carry no key for (the openers that make a
  sentence a question, the verbs that start an instruction, the words that name a day). Each one
  reads its key from the lexicons first, so adding that key to ``rules.json`` takes the words over
  without touching the code.
- **One signature for every signal.** ``(prompt, state, lexicons) -> float``, even for the signals
  that read only the state, so ``all_signals`` can look one up by the name the rules used.

``obligation_clauses`` is the single exception to the 0.0 to 1.0 range: it returns a raw clause
count, because the rules pay it per clause with a floor and a ceiling of their own.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
from collections.abc import Mapping, Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass
from functools import lru_cache
from typing import Any, Callable

from lib import ledger

HIT = 1.0  # why: a signal that fired is a plain yes, and the weight in rules.json decides what it is worth
MISS = 0.0  # why: a signal that did not fire adds nothing, so the skin's total says only what was seen
VERY_SHORT_WORDS = 4  # why: four words or fewer is an aside, not a brief
ACK_MAX_WORDS = 3  # why: an acknowledgement is never longer than "yep sounds good"
PROGRESS_MAX_WORDS = 8  # why: a short line with a progress word in it is a progress ask; a long one is a story
DECISION_LEXICON_FULL = 2  # why: two different decision words is as sure as this signal gets, so it reads 1.0 there
LIST_MIN_CLAUSES = 3  # why: three clauses is where a sentence stops being a sentence and starts being a list
LIST_MIN_OBLIGATED = 2  # why: two obligations inside that list is what makes it requirements rather than chat

# why: phones and macOS type the curly apostrophe; every lexicon in rules.json is written with the straight one
CURLY_APOSTROPHE = "’"

# why: punctuation that sits at the edge of a word carries no meaning for a lexicon or an opener
EDGE_CHARS = " \t\r\n.,;:!?\"'()[]{}…“”‘"

# why: the member's own separators between requirements, the ones people actually type in a rant
CLAUSE_SPLIT = re.compile(r"[,;\n]+|\s+and\s+|\s+then\s+")

# why: a bare number or a mark out of ten is a rating, which is an acknowledgement with a score on it
RATING = re.compile(r"\d+(\s*/\s*10)?")

# why: an ISO date is the one date spelling that is unambiguous in every locale
ISO_DATE = re.compile(r"\b\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}\b")

# why: "by friday", "by the launch": a deadline is usually spelled with "by" and the thing it is by
BY_SOMETHING = re.compile(r"\bby\s+\w+")

# why: the words English starts a question with; the rules carry no lexicon for them, so they live here
QUESTION_OPENERS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "should", "is", "are", "can", "do", "does", "did", "which", "what",
    "how", "why", "would", "could", "will", "where", "when", "who",
)

# why: the verbs an instruction opens with; a clause that starts with one is a requirement without saying "must"
IMPERATIVE_VERBS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "add", "make", "keep", "remove", "don't", "dont", "use", "show", "open", "put", "move",
    "set", "drop", "stop", "take", "give", "fix", "build", "write", "send", "change", "get", "run",
)

# why: the markers that turn a question into a choice between named options
ALTERNATIVE_MARKERS: tuple[str, ...] = (" or ", "either", "vs", "versus", "instead")

# why: the whole prompts that mean "start", including the one that is allowed to be funny
GO_WORDS: tuple[str, ...] = ("go", "build it", "do it", "run it", "ship it", "make it so")

# why: an insult only routes to banter when it is aimed at the assistant, so this is who "you" can be
SECOND_PERSON: tuple[str, ...] = ("you", "you're", "youre", "your", "u", "claude")

# why: the words that name a day or a deadline, matched whole so "mondays" is not "monday"
DATE_WORDS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "monday", "tuesday", "wednesday", "thursday", "friday", "saturday", "sunday",
    "tomorrow", "due", "eod", "next week",
)

# why: the lexicon keys this file owns a default for; rules.json wins whenever it carries the key
ALTERNATIVE_KEY = "alternative"
GO_KEY = "go"
SECOND_PERSON_KEY = "second_person"
DATE_KEY = "date"
IMPERATIVE_KEY = "imperative"
OPENERS_KEY = "question_openers"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class State:
    """What the router knows about the build before it reads a word of the prompt."""

    ledger_on_disk: bool = False
    ledger_approved: bool = False
    build_in_flight: bool = False


def read_state() -> State:
    """The state as the ledger on disk tells it. No ledger means every answer is no."""
